<message role="assistant" ordinal="7" timestamp="2026-08-16T07:18:36.183621Z">

### What it means

`pnpm` has detected that [`/Users/amrit/fractalmandala/acrolls`](file:///Users/amrit/fractalmandala/acrolls) is a **monorepo workspace** (configured via [`pnpm-workspace.yaml`](file:///Users/amrit/fractalmandala/acrolls/pnpm-workspace.yaml)).

In a workspace, packages and applications live in subfolders (like `packages/*` or `examples/*`). By default, `pnpm` blocks running `pnpm add` at the workspace root to prevent accidentally polluting the root [`package.json`](file:///Users/amrit/fractalmandala/acrolls/package.json) with dependencies meant for a specific sub-package.

---

### How to resolve it

Choose the approach that matches your goal:

#### 1. If you meant to add it to a specific sub-package (Recommended)
You can target the specific package directly without leaving the root directory:
<code_block language="bash"><![CDATA[

# Target by package name defined in its package.json

pnpm --filter <package-name> add fractalthemer
]]></code_block>
Or `cd` into that directory first:
<code_block language="bash"><![CDATA[
cd packages/<package-name>
pnpm add fractalthemer
]]></code_block>
---

#### 2. If you intentionally want it at the workspace root (e.g., shared dev tooling)
Pass the `-w` (or `--workspace-root`) flag to tell pnpm you intentionally mean the root:
<code_block language="bash"><![CDATA[
pnpm add -w fractalthemer

# or as a devDependency:

pnpm add -w -D fractalthemer
]]></code_block>
---

#### 3. If you want to disable this warning permanently
Add this setting to your `.npmrc` file in the `acrolls` root:
<code_block language="ini"><![CDATA[
ignore-workspace-root-check=true
]]></code_block>
**Analyzing pnpm Monorepo Behavior**

<tool_result><![CDATA[
# Ogygia: SSR Islands for SvelteKit

## Purpose
Ogygia is a framework that enables selective server-side rendering with client-side interactivity in SvelteKit applications, solving the problem of shipping unnecessary JavaScript to users.

## Core Concepts

**Islands Architecture**: Rather than hydrating entire pages, developers mark specific components as interactive using import attributes. As the documentation states, "The page ships as server HTML with nothing to hydrate. Add `wake: 'load'` to an import and that component — and only that one — becomes interactive."

**Flexible Wakeup Schedules**: Components can load on different triggers—`load`, `idle`, `visible`, or media queries—allowing fine-grained control over when JavaScript arrives.

**Multiple Island Types**:
- **Client islands**: Interactive components that hydrate on demand
- **Server islands**: Per-request HTML fetched after initial paint
- **Lakes**: Static, frozen subtrees shipping zero client JavaScript
- **Held regions**: Server-selected components rendered as signed HTML

## Key Features

- ~7.6 KB runtime
- No Kit patches required
- Content collections with markdown and JSON support
- SPA router with partial prerendering
- Live partial updates via server-pushed HTML
- Cross-island state sharing through wire protocol
- View Transitions and link prefetching

## Problem Solved

Ogygia reduces JavaScript bundle sizes for mostly-static pages by decoupling interactivity from rendering, ensuring "JS ships only for the components you mark."
]]></tool_result>

<tool_result><![CDATA[
# Ogygia Collections: A Comprehensive Overview

## Core Concept

Collections in Ogygia provide a unified API for managing typed content sets with frontmatter. The framework abstracts away the underlying data source—whether markdown files, REST APIs, or CMS feeds—behind a consistent interface. As the documentation states, you "learn one API and reuse it everywhere."

## Mental Model: "The Boundary Picks the Representation"

The foundational principle is elegantly simple: **stay on the server and receive complete entries with rendered bodies; cross the wire and get plain data only.** This distinction governs all interactions:

- **Server-side** (`get`): Full entry object with body as a renderable held region
- **Over-the-wire** (`list`, `live.list`): Mapped data arrays only

## Definition Structure

Define collections once in a shared module so both server and client can access them:

```typescript
export const docs = content({
  loader: markdown(import.meta.glob('../content/docs/**/*.svx', { eager: true })),
  schema: v.object({ title: v.string(), section: v.string() }),
  filter: (e) => !e.data.draft
});
```

The three configuration parameters are:
- **`loader`**: Source specification (glob, API, CMS, or custom)
- **`schema`**: Valibot validation for frontmatter with automatic type inference
- **`filter`**: Single visibility declaration honored across all read paths

## Entry Structure: `{ id, data, meta, body }`

Every entry contains:

| Component | Description | Available Where |
|-----------|-------------|-----------------|
| **id** | Entry identifier | Everywhere |
| **data** | Validated frontmatter | Everywhere |
| **meta** | Collector output (e.g., headings) | Server only |
| **body** | Held region renderable | Server only |

## API Surface

Three primary read methods form the complete interface:

```typescript
get(id)              // Server: returns full entry or null
list({ map })        // Wire: returns mapped data array
live.get / live.list // Wire (live): re-emits on changes
```

## Markdown Pipeline

For `.svx`/`.md` files, the built-in `markdown()` loader includes:

- MDSvex processing for component imports
- Shiki syntax highlighting (configurable themes)
- Pandoc-style heading IDs (`## Title {#id}`)
- Automatic heading collection into `meta.headings` (h2–h4)

Configure globally under the Vite plugin:

```typescript
ogygia({
  content: {
    markdown: { /* themes, headingIds, headings collector config */ }
  }
});
```

## Custom Sources Pattern

Any object implementing three async methods qualifies as a source:

```typescript
const press = content({
  schema,
  loader: {
    async get(id) { /* fetch one */ },
    async list() { /* fetch all */ },
    async ids() { /* fetch ids */ }
  }
});
```

This pattern enables REST APIs, CMS integrations, or push-based feeds without changing the collection API.

## Wire Access: Remote Functions

Mint remotes in `.remote.ts` files to expose data over the wire:

```typescript
// src/lib/docs.remote.ts
export const docNav = withRemotes(docs).list({ 
  map: (e) => ({ slug: e.id, title: e.data.title }) 
});
```

The `map` function controls what data traverses the network—a critical security and performance boundary.

## Live Collections (SSE/Push)

Add a `live()` generator yielding a signal whenever the source changes:

```typescript
async *live() {
  for await (const next of subscribeToCms()) { 
    posts = next; 
    yield 1; // or yield changed ids for incremental reload
  }
}
```

Live remotes (`live.list`, `live.get`) re-emit mapped snapshots as the server receives updates, which it pushes as rendered HTML that the client morphs in place.

## Critical Implementation Patterns

**404 Handling**: Validate in the route's `load` function using `error()`, not in component-level awaits:

```typescript
// +page.ts
export async function load({ params }) {
  if (!(await docs.get(params.slug))) error(404, 'Not found');
}
```

**Client Rendering of Wire Data**: When a client must render fetched entries, map them to held regions of custom components—`<Region>` is the sole renderable crossing the wire.

**Prerendering**: Use `ids()` to generate static routes:

```typescript
export const entries = async () => (await docs.ids()).map((slug) => ({ slug }));
```

## Key Constraints

- Filters apply uniformly; remote functions can only narrow visibility, never expand it
- Bodies and metadata never cross the wire—they're server-exclusive
- Draft filtering operates everywhere automatically, including direct URL access
- Live sources require long-lived servers; serverless contexts face per-isolate session limits

This design prioritizes type safety, automatic performance optimization, and a unified developer experience across radically different content backends.
]]></tool_result>

<tool_result><![CDATA[
# Blocks in Ogygia: Concept and Implementation

## Core Mental Model

Blocks represent "data: a tree of nodes, each naming a component `type` plus its `props`." They enable content—rather than code—to determine layout. As the documentation notes, blocks are ideal when "content decides layout — marketing pages assembled in a visual editor."

The key distinction: blocks differ from hardcoded layouts because your CMS or data source controls the structure, while Ogygia handles rendering. Think of it as the visual-builder counterpart to markdown rendering.

## Architecture: Static vs. Interactive

Blocks fall into two categories determined by their import marks:

**Static blocks** use `with { region: 'raw' }`:
- Render as plain HTML/CSS with zero JavaScript
- Ship only necessary styles via code-splitting

**Interactive blocks** use `with { wake: … }`:
- Include JavaScript hydration on a schedule (`load`, `idle`, `visible`, or media queries)
- JS loads only when the page actually references that block type

## Registry Definition

Register blocks in a TypeScript module mapping type names to components:

```typescript
// src/lib/blocks.ts
import Hero    from './blocks/Hero.svelte'    with { region: 'raw' };
import Counter from './blocks/Counter.svelte' with { wake: 'load' };

export const registry = { Hero, Counter };
```

Critical principle: "You decide which component a `type` means — no guessing, no auto-discovery. An unregistered `type` is skipped with a dev warning."

## Page Schema

Pages are JSON trees with this structure:

```json
[
  { "type": "Hero", "props": { "title": "Welcome" } },
  { "type": "Grid", "children": [
    { "type": "Feature", "props": { "title": "Fast" } }
  ] }
]
```

Nesting occurs via the `children` array, creating a hierarchy rather than flat sequences.

## Rendering Pipeline

Use the `blocks()` content source:

```typescript
import { content, blocks } from 'ogygia/content';
import { registry } from '$lib/blocks';

export const pages = content({
  loader: blocks(import.meta.glob('./pages/*.json'), registry)
});
```

Render in a universal `+page.ts` load (not server-only):

```typescript
export const load = async () => ({ page: await pages.get('landing') });

// In component:
<Region of={data.page.body} />
```

**Critical constraint**: Use universal loads because "a `body` is a same-pass SSR value — a live render, not wire data."

## CMS Integration Example

For headless CMSs like Builder.io, create a loader that transforms API responses:

```typescript
const toTree = (els = []) =>
  els.filter((e) => e.component?.name).map((e) => ({
    type: e.component.name,
    props: e.component.options ?? {},
    children: e.children ? toTree(e.children) : undefined
  }));

export const pages = content({
  loader: blocks(mapRaw(builderApi, r => ({ blocks: toTree(r.data?.blocks) })), registry)
});
```

## Performance Model

The architecture ensures "the page never imports `Hero` or `Feature`, so their styles are on no page stylesheet." CSS links directly into the response during server rendering. Consequently, "a registry of 100 blocks costs a page exactly the sheets for the blocks it renders"—independent of registry size.

## Alternative: Manual Resolution

For one-off uses without collections, call `blocks.resolve(tree, registry)` server-side and implement minimal rendering logic yourself—avoiding unnecessary abstraction layers.
]]></tool_result>
</message>

# Acrolls — TECH

## Stack

- **Node** ≥ 20.19
- **pnpm** workspaces
- **Svelte 5** (runes) for `@acrolls/svelte` and Studio UI
- **SvelteKit 2.62+ / 3** for the example and Studio shell
- **mdsvex** for Markdown / `.svx` compilation
- **Shiki** for compile-time highlighting
- **TypeScript** throughout packages
- **CSS** primary styles; optional **indented SASS** token source for hosts

## Monorepo layout

```text
acrolls/
  packages/
    mdsvex/       @acrolls/mdsvex
    svelte/       @acrolls/svelte
    styles/       @acrolls/styles
    sveltekit/    @acrolls/sveltekit
    cli/          @acrolls/cli
  examples/
    kit-consumer/ @acrolls/example-kit
    starter/      starter article assets
  PRODUCT.md
  TECH.md
  SKILL.md
```

## Compile pipeline

```text
.md / .svx
  → mdsvex (remark-gfm + frontmatter)
  → rehype: slugs, table wrap, code frames (Shiki)
  → Svelte component graph
  → <article class="acrolls"> via Publication layout
```

The internal bundled `@acrolls/mdsvex` unit exposes its public API through `acrolls/mdsvex`,
including `createAcrollsMdsvexOptions()`, consumed by:

- host `vite.config.ts` via `acrolls/mdsvex` or `acrolls/sveltekit`
- CLI `validate` / `studio` (same options object)

## Runtime model

- Most markup is SSR-friendly static HTML from mdsvex + components.
- Client islands only where needed:
  - copy button / wrap toggle on code frames
  - image zoom dialog
  - Mermaid (lazy, post-v0 optional in v0 stub)
- No compiler deps in browser bundles (Shiki stays compile-time).

## Style architecture

- `.acrolls` publication boundary
- CSS variables `--acrolls-*` with host fallbacks (`--font-body`, `--foreground`, …)
=== sveltekit pkg ===
index.ts
=== docs pkg ===

<message role="assistant" ordinal="15" timestamp="2026-08-14T15:07:30.275Z" has_thinking="true">
<thinking><![CDATA[
I have enough context now to synthesize a comparison. Acrolls is a SvelteKit publishing/docs framework centered on the mdsvex compile pipeline, editorial primitives like Publication, Banner, Callout, and Figure, a docs shell with nav/TOC/breadcrumbs/pager, CLI onboarding, and corpus validation, with its content model adapting Vite globs into a unified docs content source.

Ogygia, by contrast, is an SSR islands framework offering wake-scheduled islands, a valibot-based content collections API, JSON-tree blocks mapped to a component registry, remote function boundaries, live SSE-driven collections, and held regions.

The real question is whether acrolls could meaningfully absorb Ogygia's collections and blocks ideas without becoming scope-creepy or philosophically misaligned — acrolls deliberately stays conservative and lets you own the SvelteKit app, while Ogygia is a more invasive runtime that reshapes how SvelteKit renders, so wholesale adoption would clash, though some underlying concepts could still be worth borrowing without importing the whole rendering model.

The clearest win is collections ergonomics: acrolls currently forces hosts to maintain two parallel globs plus a contentRoot prefix and config object, which is exactly the friction a single `content()`/`collection()` factory with a loader abstraction would eliminate, and pairing that with valibot-style schema validation would give typed frontmatter inference alongside the existing diagnostics. A unified declarative `filter` applied consistently across nav, breadcrumbs, pager, routes, and static entries would also be cleaner than the current scattered draft/hidden metadata handling.

I also like reframing acrolls' entry shape around a single `{ id, data, meta, body }` type with the "boundary picks the representation" idea — they already separate lazy body from eager metadata, so this would just make that insight more explicit. And a pluggable custom source pattern (`get/list/ids`) would give acrolls a clean seam for future CMS/API-backed content without committing to it now, since they're currently glob-only and have deferred remote content support.

Blocks, on the other hand, feels like a bigger stretch since acrolls is fundamentally a prose/docs tool rather than something built around flexible component layouts.

There's a narrower, useful slice though: a registered component allowlist for `.svx` files and structured pages like generated API references or changelog/index pages assembled from frontmatter. The explicit type→component registry with warnings for unregistered types and per-page CSS code-splitting could power these "generated" pages without hosts writing bespoke Svelte — though I'd treat this as secondary to the collections work, which is clearly higher value. The CSS code-splitting insight itself is worth noting too: a registry of many components only costs a page the styles it actually renders, which matters if acrolls' editorial primitives scale up.

As for what shouldn't be adopted: the full Ogygia runtime (held regions, wire protocol, live SSE push, SPA router) is a competing rendering framework that conflicts with acrolls' philosophy of ceding routing to the host SvelteKit app and targeting static/prerendered docs rather than long-lived servers. Blocks as a marketing-page builder is also off-mission unless tightly scoped. That said, acrolls could interoperate with Ogygia rather than absorb it.

The real takeaway is that Ogygia is a runtime you build on top of, while acrolls is a library you drop into an existing app — so "incorporating Ogygia" really means borrowing its content-modeling API design, not depending on it. I want to give a tight, prioritized answer: first frame what each tool is and where they diverge, then lay out what's worth borrowing.
]]></thinking>
I&#39;ve now got both sides clearly. Here&#39;s my assessment.

## The core mismatch to resolve first

These two projects sit at **different layers**, and that determines what &#34;incorporate&#34; can safely mean:

- **Ogygia is a runtime framework you build *on*** — it owns rendering (SSR islands, held regions, a wire protocol, lakes, an SPA router, live SSE push). It ~patches how SvelteKit hydrates.
- **Acrolls is a library you drop *into* an existing SvelteKit app.** Its stated boundary (PRODUCT.md:17-24) explicitly *cedes* routing, deployment, and runtime to the host: &#34;You own the SvelteKit app.&#34;

So you should **not** adopt Ogygia as a dependency or absorb its runtime — that would break the &#34;installs into any vanilla SvelteKit host, no Kit patches&#34; promise that is acrolls&#39; whole positioning. What&#39;s worth taking is **Ogygia&#39;s content-modeling API design**, which is genuinely better than what acrolls exposes today, and which requires none of Ogygia&#39;s runtime.

## What&#39;s genuinely worth borrowing (prioritized)

### 1. The `content()` collection API — high value, directly fixes existing friction

Acrolls&#39; content source today makes the host author **two parallel globs** keyed identically, plus a facts glob, plus a `contentRoot` prefix, plus a separate `DocsContentConfig` (`packages/sveltekit/src/index.ts:33-70`):
<code_block language="ts"><![CDATA[
modules  = import.meta.glob('../content/**/*.md');
metadata = import.meta.glob('../content/**/*.md', { eager: true, import: 'metadata' });
facts    = import.meta.glob('../content/**/*.md', { eager: true, import: '__acrollsDocument' });
createAcrollsDocsSource({ modules, metadata, facts, contentRoot, config });
]]></code_block>
Ogygia collapses that to one declaration:
<code_block language="ts"><![CDATA[
export const docs = content({
  loader: markdown(import.meta.glob('../content/docs/**/*.svx', { eager: true })),
  schema: v.object({ title: v.string(), section: v.string() }),
  filter: (e) => !e.data.draft,
});
]]></code_block>
Three specific things acrolls should lift:

- **A single loader that internally derives lazy body + eager metadata**, so the host writes *one* glob. Acrolls already splits &#34;lazy body vs. eager facts&#34; — that&#39;s literally Ogygia&#39;s *&#34;the boundary picks the representation&#34;* insight (body/meta are server-only; data crosses the wire). You&#39;re doing the same thing with worse ergonomics. Reframing the public API around a single `{ id, data, meta, body }` entry would clarify it without changing the compile pipeline underneath.
- **Standard-schema (Valibot/Zod) frontmatter validation with type inference.** Acrolls already has a corpus-preflight/diagnostics system (PRODUCT.md:229-298) but gives the host *no typed schema* for frontmatter. A `schema:` field folds cleanly into your existing `authored`/`migration` modes — schema failure becomes just another diagnostic code — and gives hosts inferred `data` types for free.
- **One declarative `filter` honored across every read path** (nav, breadcrumbs, pager, routes, static entries). Acrolls currently expresses visibility as per-doc `hidden`/`draft` metadata scattered across behaviors 15 and 12. Ogygia&#39;s &#34;filter narrows everywhere, uniformly, including direct URL access&#34; is a cleaner contract and matches acrolls&#39; determinism goals.

### 2. The pluggable loader / custom-source seam — high value, low commitment

Ogygia&#39;s source interface is just `{ get, list, ids }`. That&#39;s the exact seam acrolls needs for the &#34;remote content&#34; it currently **defers** (PRODUCT.md:118). If your loader is an interface rather than hardcoded glob-adapting, a host can later back docs with a CMS/API **without changing any of your nav/route/breadcrumb/pager generation** — because those consume `ids()`/`list()`/`get()`, not globs. You don&#39;t have to build remote sources now; you just stop making glob the only possible loader.

### 3. Per-component CSS code-splitting — medium value, on-ethos

Ogygia&#39;s nice property: &#34;a registry of 100 blocks costs a page exactly the sheets for the blocks it renders.&#34; Acrolls ships editorial primitives (Video, Mermaid, Callout, Figure). Check whether `default.css` is monolithic — if a docs page with no video still pays for video CSS, moving to component-linked styles is an efficiency win that fits your existing &#34;client islands only where needed&#34; runtime model (TECH.md).

### 4. Declarative wake vocabulary — low value, cosmetic

Acrolls already does ad-hoc islands (copy button, zoom dialog, lazy Mermaid). Ogygia&#39;s `wake: &#39;load&#39; | &#39;idle&#39; | &#39;visible&#39; | media` is just nicer vocabulary for what you hand-roll. Worth borrowing the *naming/pattern* (Mermaid → `visible`, zoom → `idle`), **not** the runtime. Minor.

## What to skip (and why)

- **Blocks (JSON tree → component registry).** This is a *marketing-page / visual-builder* feature — &#34;content decides layout.&#34; Acrolls is a **prose and docs** publisher; that&#39;s off-mission. The one narrow slice that could apply: a registered component allowlist to power *generated* pages (a docs landing/category index assembled from frontmatter, an auto-built changelog). If you want that, take only the registry idea (explicit `type → component`, unregistered type skipped with a dev warning) — not the full blocks system.
- **The Ogygia runtime**: held regions, wire protocol, lakes, SPA router, and especially **live/SSE collections** (which require long-lived servers). Acrolls targets static/prerendered docs and cedes runtime to the host. Adopting any of this violates your responsibility boundary and the &#34;any SvelteKit host&#34; contract.

## Bottom line

Yes — acrolls can be meaningfully improved, but by **borrowing Ogygia&#39;s content API design, not its framework**. The single highest-leverage move is replacing the triple-glob `createAcrollsDocsSource` with a `content({ loader, schema, filter })` factory over a pluggable `{ get, list, ids }` loader and a single `{ id, data, meta, body }` entry type. That one change: cuts host boilerplate to a third, adds typed frontmatter, unifies your visibility rules, and opens the CMS/remote seam you&#39;ve already deferred — all without touching the mdsvex pipeline or crossing your &#34;host owns the runtime&#34; line.

Want me to draft the concrete API surface for the reworked `content()` source (types + a migration shim from the current `createAcrollsDocsSource`), so you can see it against the existing code before committing?

<thinking><![CDATA[
With the full picture of the existing content source in mind, I'm drafting a reworked `content()` API as a thin, ergonomic façade over `createDocsContentSource`, adding a loader abstraction, schema-based frontmatter validation feeding diagnostics, a filter predicate, a unified entry type, and a migration path from the old approach.

I need to bridge the existing synchronous eager-metadata/lazy-body source with an async `get()` model, keeping sync nav/document generation for SSR while layering async `get(id)`, `list()`, and `ids()` methods on top, plus schema validation at construction time and uniform filtering. I'm working out how to map identity between the old `document.slug`/`key` and the new `entry.id`, leaning toward using slug as the public routing id while preserving key internally for filesystem stability.

The design should stay a façade over the existing content source rather than a rewrite, keep the loader pluggable with a glob adapter as default, type schema validation against the Standard Schema interface so acrolls doesn't hard-depend on any one validation library, and support a filter predicate over id/data/meta. Given the user wants a concrete API surface to compare against existing code, I should draft an actual TypeScript file defining the new public interface.

I'll write this as a scratchpad proposal rather than editing the real package, since the user wants to review before committing. The draft needs to define the core types, a content() adapter that maps to the existing source creator, a markdownGlob() loader to replace the repetitive glob setup, and a compatibility shim for the old API built on top of content(). I also need to think through the async boundary carefully, since the existing source creator works synchronously with pre-materialized documents while a custom loader might need to be async.

To keep the existing shim drop-in compatible, I realize I should keep a synchronous path for eager glob loaders since that's what it already relies on. I'm sketching a loader interface that distinguishes eager from async loading, with a `load()` method that can return either raw inputs directly or a promise of them.

Looking at how Ogygia structures its loaders with `get`, `list`, and `ids`, I see this maps well onto the docs use case: `list()` can supply metadata for all documents to build the navigation tree, while `get(id)` fetches the full body for a single route. This separation between listing metadata and fetching full content fits the acrolls docs pattern perfectly, so I'm defining the loader interface around that same split.

I'm working out that `get` and `ids` can simply be derived from `list()` for the glob-based case, keeping the engine simple since navigation always needs all metadata anyway -- though a remote loader could implement them directly later for efficiency. Now I'm turning to the `markdown()` glob adapter, which needs three separate glob imports for lazy bodies, eager frontmatter, and eager computed facts, since Vite's import.meta.glob requires distinct calls for each import mode.

Actually, I realize I can cut it down to two glob calls instead of three by using a single eager glob without an import filter, then reading both `.metadata` and `.__acrollsDocument` off each module — combining what used to be separate metadata and facts globs into one. That's a genuine ergonomic improvement worth pursuing.

Next I need to think through schema validation: running each doc's data through StandardSchema, and on failure emitting a diagnostic with code ACROLLS_SCHEMA_INVALID, with rejection behavior depending on the mode.

For filtering, I'm deciding that `filter` should remove documents entirely from all read paths — nav, routes, get, ids — rather than just marking them hidden like unlisted-but-routeable drafts. The cleanest way is to exclude filtered inputs from the documents array before it reaches the engine, giving uniform removal everywhere. Now I want to start drafting a scratchpad TypeScript file to work through this.

Sketching out the draft file's structure...

For the migration shim, I'm reworking createAcrollsDocsSource to delegate to the new content() factory, with markdownGlob needing to support both a single eager module glob and pre-split metadata/facts inputs for backward compatibility.

That internal wrapper would take a ready array of content inputs, letting both the shim and custom sources reuse it. The shim then builds inputs exactly as it does today and passes them through unchanged, keeping its behavior byte-identical while the new `content()`/`markdownGlob()` becomes the front door. Time to write it out.
]]></thinking>
This is a clean façade opportunity — the existing `createDocsContentSource` engine (nav/breadcrumb/pager/route generation, diagnostics, admission modes) is solid and worth keeping untouched. The rework is a new ergonomic front door over it, plus a schema/filter/loader layer. Let me write the draft as a proposal file so you can read it against the real code without touching the package.

{"file_path":"/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-amrit-fractalmandala/644df3ae-a451-4be2-a03f-e4443e3d07b4/scratchpad/acrolls-content-api-draft.ts","content":"/**\n * PROPOSAL DRAFT — `acrolls/content` reworked source API.\n *\n * Goal: replace the triple-glob `createAcrollsDocsSource(...)` host boilerplate with a\n * single Ogygia-style `content({ loader, schema, filter })` declaration, WITHOUT touching\n * the existing engine (`createDocsContentSource`) or the mdsvex/nav/route generation.\n *\n * This is a thin façade + three additions:\n *   1. A `loader` seam  → glob today, custom `{ list }` source (CMS/API) later.\n *   2. A `schema`       → Standard Schema frontmatter validation + inferred `data` types.\n *   3. A `filter`       → one predicate honored across every read path (uniform removal).\n *\n * Entry shape unifies acrolls' \"eager metadata vs lazy body\" split into Ogygia's\n * `{ id, data, meta, body }` — \"the boundary picks the representation\":\n *   - data : validated frontmatter        — everywhere (safe to cross the wire)\n *   - meta : collector output (headings)  — server only\n *   - body : renderable (lazy component)  — server only\n *\n * Nothing here requires Ogygia's runtime (islands, held regions, wire protocol). It is a\n * design borrow, not a dependency. Lives inside vanilla SvelteKit, per acrolls' boundary.\n */\n\nimport {\n\tcreateDocsContentSource,\n\ttype DocsContentConfig,\n\ttype DocsContentInput,\n\ttype DocsContentSource,\n\ttype DocsContentDocument,\n\ttype DocsContentDiagnostic,\n\ttype DocsMetadata,\n\ttype DocsDocumentFacts\n} from '@acrolls/docs/content';\n\n/* ------------------------------------------------------------------ *\n * 1. Standard Schema — bring-your-own validator (Valibot/Zod/Arktype)\n *    Acrolls does NOT hard-depend on Valibot. It types against the\n *    `~standard` interface, so the host picks the library.\n * ------------------------------------------------------------------ */\n\nexport interface StandardSchemaV1\u003cInput = unknown, Output = Input\u003e {\n\treadonly '~standard': {\n\t\treadonly version: 1;\n\t\treadonly vendor: string;\n\t\treadonly validate: (value: unknown) =\u003e\n\t\t\t| { readonly value: Output; readonly issues?: undefined }\n\t\t\t| { readonly issues: ReadonlyArray\u003c{ readonly message: string; readonly path?: ReadonlyArray\u003cPropertyKey | { key: PropertyKey }\u003e }\u003e };\n\t};\n}\ntype InferOutput\u003cS\u003e = S extends StandardSchemaV1\u003cany, infer O\u003e ? O : never;\n\n/* ------------------------------------------------------------------ *\n * 2. The unified entry — what a host actually thinks in terms of.\n * ------------------------------------------------------------------ */\n\n/** Full entry — server-side (`get`). `meta`/`body` never cross the wire. */\nexport type Entry\u003cTData, TDocument\u003e = {\n\t/** Public routeable identity (the doc slug; '' is the base landing). */\n\treadonly id: string;\n\t/** Source filesystem key, e.g. \"guide/intro.md\" — stable across renames of the slug. */\n\treadonly key: string;\n\t/** Validated frontmatter. Typed from `schema` when provided, else `DocsMetadata`. */\n\treadonly data: TData;\n\t/** Collector output (headings h2–h4, hasFrontmatter, links). Server only. */\n\treadonly meta?: DocsDocumentFacts;\n\t/** Lazy renderable body (the mdsvex component module). Server only. */\n\treadonly body: () =\u003e Promise\u003cTDocument\u003e;\n\treadonly href: string;\n\treadonly hidden: boolean;\n};\n\n/** Wire-safe projection — what `list({ map })` returns. Body/meta intentionally absent. */\nexport type EntrySummary\u003cTData\u003e = {\n\treadonly id: string;\n\treadonly key: string;\n\treadonly data: TData;\n\treadonly href: string;\n\treadonly hidden: boolean;\n};\n\n/* ------------------------------------------------------------------ *\n * 3. The loader seam — the single extension point.\n *    A loader only has to produce the list of documents. `get`/`ids`\n *    are derived (nav needs every doc's metadata anyway). A remote\n *    loader may add `live()` later without changing this API.\n * ------------------------------------------------------------------ */\n\n/** One raw document a loader yields: frontmatter (eager) + lazy body. */\nexport type LoadedDocument\u003cTDocument\u003e = {\n\tkey: string;\n\tdata: DocsMetadata;\n\tmeta?: DocsDocumentFacts;\n\tload: () =\u003e Promise\u003cTDocument\u003e;\n};\n\nexport interface ContentLoader\u003cTDocument\u003e {\n\t/** Eager for globs; async for CMS/API. Nav requires all metadata regardless. */\n\tlist(): LoadedDocument\u003cTDocument\u003e[] | Promise\u003cLoadedDocument\u003cTDocument\u003e[]\u003e;\n\t/** True when `list()` is synchronous — enables the sync migration shim. */\n\treadonly eager: boolean;\n\t/** Post-v0: yields on source change for live remotes (SSE/push). */\n\tlive?(): AsyncIterable\u003cnumber | ReadonlyArray\u003cstring\u003e\u003e;\n}\n\n/* ---- 3a. The glob loader: two globs, one pattern (down from three) ---- */\n/*\n * Vite constraint: keeping bodies lazy needs one lazy glob (the `default`\n * component) AND one eager glob (frontmatter). We read BOTH `metadata` and\n * `__acrollsDocument` off the single eager module, so the host writes the\n * pattern twice, not three times. Honest limit: Vite can't get this to one.\n */\nexport type MarkdownGlobOptions\u003cTDocument\u003e = {\n\t/** import.meta.glob('../content/**\\/*.md')                — lazy component modules */\n\tbody: Record\u003cstring, () =\u003e Promise\u003cTDocument\u003e\u003e;\n\t/** import.meta.glob('../content/**\\/*.md', { eager: true }) — full eager modules */\n\tmodules: Record\u003cstring, { metadata?: DocsMetadata; __acrollsDocument?: DocsDocumentFacts }\u003e;\n\t/** Directory prefix stripped from glob keys, e.g. \"../content\". */\n\troot: string;\n};\n\nexport function markdownGlob\u003cTDocument\u003e(opts: MarkdownGlobOptions\u003cTDocument\u003e): ContentLoader\u003cTDocument\u003e {\n\tconst root = normalizeGlobPath(opts.root);\n\treturn {\n\t\teager: true,\n\t\tlist() {\n\t\t\treturn Object.entries(opts.body).map(([globKey, load]) =\u003e {\n\t\t\t\tconst mod = opts.modules[globKey];\n\t\t\t\treturn {\n\t\t\t\t\tkey: removeGlobRoot(globKey, root),\n\t\t\t\t\tdata: mod?.metadata ?? {},\n\t\t\t\t\tmeta: mod?.__acrollsDocument,\n\t\t\t\t\tload\n\t\t\t\t};\n\t\t\t});\n\t\t}\n\t};\n}\n\n/* ---- 3b. Custom source: the CMS/API/remote seam (deferred content) ---- */\n/*\n * Any object producing LoadedDocument[] qualifies. The nav/route engine is\n * unchanged — it consumes list() output, not globs. This is the clean seam\n * for the \"remote content\" PRODUCT.md defers, with zero commitment now.\n */\nexport function customSource\u003cTDocument\u003e(\n\tsource: Pick\u003cContentLoader\u003cTDocument\u003e, 'list' | 'live'\u003e\n): ContentLoader\u003cTDocument\u003e {\n\treturn { eager: false, list: source.list, live: source.live };\n}\n\n/* ------------------------------------------------------------------ *\n * 4. content() — the new front door.\n * ------------------------------------------------------------------ */\n\nexport type ContentOptions\u003cTDocument, TSchema extends StandardSchemaV1 | undefined\u003e = {\n\tloader: ContentLoader\u003cTDocument\u003e;\n\t/** Host-owned information architecture (unchanged existing config object). */\n\tconfig: DocsContentConfig;\n\t/** Optional frontmatter schema. Failures become diagnostics (see mode). */\n\tschema?: TSchema;\n\t/**\n\t * One visibility predicate honored across nav, routes, get, list, ids, static\n\t * entries — uniform *removal* (unlike `hidden`, which stays routeable/unlisted).\n\t * Runs against validated data, so `e.data.draft` is typed.\n\t */\n\tfilter?: (e: { id: string; key: string; data: TData\u003cTSchema\u003e; meta?: DocsDocumentFacts }) =\u003e boolean;\n};\n\ntype TData\u003cTSchema\u003e = TSchema extends StandardSchemaV1 ? InferOutput\u003cTSchema\u003e : DocsMetadata;\n\nexport type Collection\u003cTDocument, TSchema extends StandardSchemaV1 | undefined\u003e = {\n\t/** Async resolve → the existing engine's source (nav, diagnostics, get/load/entries). */\n\tsource(): Promise\u003cDocsContentSource\u003cTDocument\u003e\u003e;\n\t/** Sync resolve — only for eager (glob) loaders. Throws on async loaders. */\n\tsourceSync(): DocsContentSource\u003cTDocument\u003e;\n\n\t/* Ogygia-style read surface, layered on the engine: */\n\t/** Server: full entry (data+meta+body) or null. Use in route load for 404s. */\n\tget(id: string): Promise\u003cEntry\u003cTData\u003cTSchema\u003e, TDocument\u003e | null\u003e;\n\t/** Wire: mapped summaries only — body/meta never included. */\n\tlist\u003cR\u003e(opts?: { map?: (e: EntrySummary\u003cTData\u003cTSchema\u003e\u003e) =\u003e R }): Promise\u003cR[]\u003e;\n\t/** Prerender helper: `export const entries = () =\u003e col.ids().then(...)`. */\n\tids(): Promise\u003cstring[]\u003e;\n};\n\nexport function content\u003cTDocument, TSchema extends StandardSchemaV1 | undefined = undefined\u003e(\n\toptions: ContentOptions\u003cTDocument, TSchema\u003e\n): Collection\u003cTDocument, TSchema\u003e {\n\ttype Data = TData\u003cTSchema\u003e;\n\n\t/** Validate + filter, then hand the survivors to the untouched engine. */\n\tconst buildInputs = (\n\t\tloaded: LoadedDocument\u003cTDocument\u003e[]\n\t): { inputs: DocsContentInput\u003cTDocument\u003e[]; diagnostics: DocsContentDiagnostic[] } =\u003e {\n\t\tconst inputs: DocsContentInput\u003cTDocument\u003e[] = [];\n\t\tconst diagnostics: DocsContentDiagnostic[] = [];\n\n\t\tfor (const doc of loaded) {\n\t\t\t// 4a. schema validation → typed data or a diagnostic.\n\t\t\tlet data = doc.data as Data;\n\t\t\tif (options.schema) {\n\t\t\t\tconst result = options.schema['~standard'].validate(doc.data);\n\t\t\t\tif (result.issues) {\n\t\t\t\t\tdiagnostics.push({\n\t\t\t\t\t\tcode: 'ACROLLS_SCHEMA_INVALID',\n\t\t\t\t\t\tseverity: 'error',\n\t\t\t\t\t\tfile: doc.key,\n\t\t\t\t\t\tmessage: `Frontmatter failed schema: ${result.issues.map((i) =\u003e i.message).join('; ')}`,\n\t\t\t\t\t\tremediation: 'Fix the frontmatter fields to match the collection schema.'\n\t\t\t\t\t});\n\t\t\t\t\t// authored mode: skip (rejected). migration mode: keep raw, still surface the diagnostic.\n\t\t\t\t\tif (options.config.convention?.mode === 'authored') continue;\n\t\t\t\t} else {\n\t\t\t\t\tdata = result.value as Data;\n\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t}\n\n\t\t\t// 4b. filter → uniform removal (gone from every read path, unlike `hidden`).\n\t\t\tif (options.filter \u0026\u0026 !options.filter({ id: keyToId(doc.key), key: doc.key, data, meta: doc.meta })) {\n\t\t\t\tcontinue;\n\t\t\t}\n\n\t\t\tinputs.push({ key: doc.key, metadata: data as DocsMetadata, facts: doc.meta, load: doc.load });\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn { inputs, diagnostics };\n\t};\n\n\tconst makeSource = (loaded: LoadedDocument\u003cTDocument\u003e[]): DocsContentSource\u003cTDocument\u003e =\u003e {\n\t\tconst { inputs, diagnostics } = buildInputs(loaded);\n\t\tconst engine = createDocsContentSource({ config: options.config, documents: inputs });\n\t\t// Merge schema/filter diagnostics with the engine's admission diagnostics.\n\t\treturn { ...engine, diagnostics: [...diagnostics, ...engine.diagnostics] };\n\t};\n\n\tconst toEntry = (doc: DocsContentDocument\u003cTDocument\u003e): Entry\u003cData, TDocument\u003e =\u003e ({\n\t\tid: doc.slug,\n\t\tkey: doc.key,\n\t\tdata: doc.metadata as Data,\n\t\tmeta: doc.facts,\n\t\tbody: doc.loader,\n\t\thref: doc.href,\n\t\thidden: doc.hidden\n\t});\n\n\tconst resolve = async () =\u003e makeSource(await options.loader.list());\n\n\treturn {\n\t\tsource: resolve,\n\t\tsourceSync() {\n\t\t\tif (!options.loader.eager) {\n\t\t\t\tthrow new Error('sourceSync() requires an eager loader (markdownGlob). Use await source() for custom/remote sources.');\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\treturn makeSource(options.loader.list() as LoadedDocument\u003cTDocument\u003e[]);\n\t\t},\n\t\tasync get(id) {\n\t\t\tconst src = await resolve();\n\t\t\tconst doc = src.get(id);\n\t\t\treturn doc ? toEntry(doc) : null;\n\t\t},\n\t\tasync list(opts) {\n\t\t\tconst src = await resolve();\n\t\t\tconst summaries: EntrySummary\u003cData\u003e[] = src.documents\n\t\t\t\t.filter((d) =\u003e !d.hidden)\n\t\t\t\t.map((d) =\u003e ({ id: d.slug, key: d.key, data: d.metadata as Data, href: d.href, hidden: d.hidden }));\n\t\t\treturn (opts?.map ? summaries.map(opts.map) : (summaries as unknown[])) as any;\n\t\t},\n\t\tasync ids() {\n\t\t\tconst src = await resolve();\n\t\t\treturn src.documents.map((d) =\u003e d.slug);\n\t\t}\n\t};\n}\n\n/* ------------------------------------------------------------------ *\n * 5. Migration shim — createAcrollsDocsSource() unchanged for hosts.\n *    Re-expressed over content()+an inputs loader so old and new share\n *    ONE engine path. Behavior is byte-identical (eager, sync, same\n *    config) → lowest-risk migration; hosts move to content() at will.\n * ------------------------------------------------------------------ */\n\nexport type AcrollsDocsSourceOptions\u003cTDocument\u003e = {\n\tmodules: Record\u003cstring, () =\u003e Promise\u003cTDocument\u003e\u003e;\n\tmetadata?: Record\u003cstring, DocsMetadata\u003e;\n\tfacts?: Record\u003cstring, DocsDocumentFacts\u003e;\n\tcontentRoot: string;\n\tconfig: DocsContentConfig;\n};\n\n/** @deprecated Prefer content({ loader: markdownGlob(...) }). Kept for existing hosts. */\nexport function createAcrollsDocsSource\u003cTDocument\u003e(\n\toptions: AcrollsDocsSourceOptions\u003cTDocument\u003e\n): DocsContentSource\u003cTDocument\u003e {\n\tconst root = normalizeGlobPath(options.contentRoot);\n\tconst inputsLoader: ContentLoader\u003cTDocument\u003e = {\n\t\teager: true,\n\t\tlist: () =\u003e\n\t\t\tObject.entries(options.modules).map(([key, load]) =\u003e ({\n\t\t\t\tkey: removeGlobRoot(key, root),\n\t\t\t\tdata: options.metadata?.[key] ?? {},\n\t\t\t\tmeta: options.facts?.[key],\n\t\t\t\tload\n\t\t\t}))\n\t};\n\treturn content({ loader: inputsLoader, config: options.config }).sourceSync();\n}\n\n/* ------------------------------------------------------------------ *\n * helpers (mirrors of the existing sveltekit index normalizers)\n * ------------------------------------------------------------------ */\nfunction normalizeGlobPath(v: string): string {\n\treturn v.replaceAll('\\\\', '/').replace(/^\\.\\//, '').replace(/\\/+$/, '');\n}\nfunction removeGlobRoot(key: string, root: string): string {\n\tconst k = key.replaceAll('\\\\', '/');\n\tconst prefix = `${root}/`;\n\tif (!k.startsWith(prefix)) throw new Error(`Docs glob key \"${key}\" is outside contentRoot \"${root}\".`);\n\treturn k.slice(prefix.length);\n}\nfunction keyToId(key: string): string {\n\t// Rough id preview for the filter predicate; the engine remains the route authority.\n\treturn key.replace(/\\.md$/, '').replace(/\\/index$/, '').replace(/^index$/, '');\n}\n"}

@@ -0,0 +1,322 @@
+/**
+ * PROPOSAL DRAFT — `acrolls/content` reworked source API.
+ *
+ * Goal: replace the triple-glob `createAcrollsDocsSource(...)` host boilerplate with a
+ * single Ogygia-style `content({ loader, schema, filter })` declaration, WITHOUT touching
+ * the existing engine (`createDocsContentSource`) or the mdsvex/nav/route generation.
+ *
+ * This is a thin façade + three additions:
+ *   1. A `loader` seam  → glob today, custom `{ list }` source (CMS/API) later.
+ *   2. A `schema`       → Standard Schema frontmatter validation + inferred `data` types.
+ *   3. A `filter`       → one predicate honored across every read path (uniform removal).
+ *
+ * Entry shape unifies acrolls' "eager metadata vs lazy body" split into Ogygia's
+ * `{ id, data, meta, body }` — "the boundary picks the representation":
+ *   - data : validated frontmatter        — everywhere (safe to cross the wire)
+ *   - meta : collector output (headings)  — server only
+ *   - body : renderable (lazy component)  — server only
+ *
+ * Nothing here requires Ogygia's runtime (islands, held regions, wire protocol). It is a
+ * design borrow, not a dependency. Lives inside vanilla SvelteKit, per acrolls' boundary.
+ */
+
+import {
+	createDocsContentSource,
+	type DocsContentConfig,
+	type DocsContentInput,
+	type DocsContentSource,
+	type DocsContentDocument,
+	type DocsContentDiagnostic,
+	type DocsMetadata,
+	type DocsDocumentFacts
+} from '@acrolls/docs/content';
+
+/* ------------------------------------------------------------------ *
+ * 1. Standard Schema — bring-your-own validator (Valibot/Zod/Arktype)
+ *    Acrolls does NOT hard-depend on Valibot. It types against the
+ *    `~standard` interface, so the host picks the library.
+ * ------------------------------------------------------------------ */
+
+export interface StandardSchemaV1<Input = unknown, Output = Input> {
+	readonly '~standard': {
+		readonly version: 1;
+		readonly vendor: string;
+		readonly validate: (value: unknown) =>
+			| { readonly value: Output; readonly issues?: undefined }
+			| { readonly issues: ReadonlyArray<{ readonly message: string; readonly path?: ReadonlyArray<PropertyKey | { key: PropertyKey }> }> };
+	};
+}
+type InferOutput<S> = S extends StandardSchemaV1<any, infer O> ? O : never;
+
+/* ------------------------------------------------------------------ *
+ * 2. The unified entry — what a host actually thinks in terms of.
+ * ------------------------------------------------------------------ */
+
+/** Full entry — server-side (`get`). `meta`/`body` never cross the wire. */
+export type Entry<TData, TDocument> = {
+	/** Public routeable identity (the doc slug; '' is the base landing). */
+	readonly id: string;
+	/** Source filesystem key, e.g. "guide/intro.md" — stable across renames of the slug. */
+	readonly key: string;
+	/** Validated frontmatter. Typed from `schema` when provided, else `DocsMetadata`. */
+	readonly data: TData;
+	/** Collector output (headings h2–h4, hasFrontmatter, links). Server only. */
+	readonly meta?: DocsDocumentFacts;
+	/** Lazy renderable body (the mdsvex component module). Server only. */
+	readonly body: () => Promise<TDocument>;
+	readonly href: string;
+	readonly hidden: boolean;
+};
+
+/** Wire-safe projection — what `list({ map })` returns. Body/meta intentionally absent. */
+export type EntrySummary<TData> = {
+	readonly id: string;
+	readonly key: string;
+	readonly data: TData;
+	readonly href: string;
+	readonly hidden: boolean;
+};
+
+/* ------------------------------------------------------------------ *
+ * 3. The loader seam — the single extension point.
+ *    A loader only has to produce the list of documents. `get`/`ids`
+ *    are derived (nav needs every doc's metadata anyway). A remote
+ *    loader may add `live()` later without changing this API.
+ * ------------------------------------------------------------------ */
+
+/** One raw document a loader yields: frontmatter (eager) + lazy body. */
+export type LoadedDocument<TDocument> = {
+	key: string;
+	data: DocsMetadata;
+	meta?: DocsDocumentFacts;
+	load: () => Promise<TDocument>;
+};
+
+export interface ContentLoader<TDocument> {
+	/** Eager for globs; async for CMS/API. Nav requires all metadata regardless. */
+	list(): LoadedDocument<TDocument>[] | Promise<LoadedDocument<TDocument>[]>;
+	/** True when `list()` is synchronous — enables the sync migration shim. */
+	readonly eager: boolean;
+	/** Post-v0: yields on source change for live remotes (SSE/push). */
+	live?(): AsyncIterable<number | ReadonlyArray<string>>;
+}
+
+/* ---- 3a. The glob loader: two globs, one pattern (down from three) ---- */
+/*
+ * Vite constraint: keeping bodies lazy needs one lazy glob (the `default`
+ * component) AND one eager glob (frontmatter). We read BOTH `metadata` and
+ * `__acrollsDocument` off the single eager module, so the host writes the
+ * pattern twice, not three times. Honest limit: Vite can't get this to one.
+ */
+export type MarkdownGlobOptions<TDocument> = {
+	/** import.meta.glob('../content/**\/*.md')                — lazy component modules */
+	body: Record<string, () => Promise<TDocument>>;
+	/** import.meta.glob('../content/**\/*.md', { eager: true }) — full eager modules */
+	modules: Record<string, { metadata?: DocsMetadata; __acrollsDocument?: DocsDocumentFacts }>;
+	/** Directory prefix stripped from glob keys, e.g. "../content". */
+	root: string;
+};
+
+export function markdownGlob<TDocument>(opts: MarkdownGlobOptions<TDocument>): ContentLoader<TDocument> {
+	const root = normalizeGlobPath(opts.root);
+	return {
+		eager: true,
+		list() {
+			return Object.entries(opts.body).map(([globKey, load]) => {
+				const mod = opts.modules[globKey];
+				return {
+					key: removeGlobRoot(globKey, root),
+					data: mod?.metadata ?? {},
+					meta: mod?.__acrollsDocument,
+					load
+				};
+			});
+		}
+	};
+}
+
+/* ---- 3b. Custom source: the CMS/API/remote seam (deferred content) ---- */
+/*
+ * Any object producing LoadedDocument[] qualifies. The nav/route engine is
+ * unchanged — it consumes list() output, not globs. This is the clean seam
+ * for the "remote content" PRODUCT.md defers, with zero commitment now.
+ */
+export function customSource<TDocument>(
+	source: Pick<ContentLoader<TDocument>, 'list' | 'live'>
+): ContentLoader<TDocument> {
+	return { eager: false, list: source.list, live: source.live };
+}
+
+/* ------------------------------------------------------------------ *
+ * 4. content() — the new front door.
+ * ------------------------------------------------------------------ */
+
+export type ContentOptions<TDocument, TSchema extends StandardSchemaV1 | undefined> = {
+	loader: ContentLoader<TDocument>;
+	/** Host-owned information architecture (unchanged existing config object). */
+	config: DocsContentConfig;
+	/** Optional frontmatter schema. Failures become diagnostics (see mode). */
+	schema?: TSchema;
+	/**
+	 * One visibility predicate honored across nav, routes, get, list, ids, static
+	 * entries — uniform *removal* (unlike `hidden`, which stays routeable/unlisted).
+	 * Runs against validated data, so `e.data.draft` is typed.
+	 */
+	filter?: (e: { id: string; key: string; data: TData<TSchema>; meta?: DocsDocumentFacts }) => boolean;
+};
+
+type TData<TSchema> = TSchema extends StandardSchemaV1 ? InferOutput<TSchema> : DocsMetadata;
+
+export type Collection<TDocument, TSchema extends StandardSchemaV1 | undefined> = {
+	/** Async resolve → the existing engine's source (nav, diagnostics, get/load/entries). */
+	source(): Promise<DocsContentSource<TDocument>>;
+	/** Sync resolve — only for eager (glob) loaders. Throws on async loaders. */
+	sourceSync(): DocsContentSource<TDocument>;
+
+	/* Ogygia-style read surface, layered on the engine: */
+	/** Server: full entry (data+meta+body) or null. Use in route load for 404s. */
+	get(id: string): Promise<Entry<TData<TSchema>, TDocument> | null>;
+	/** Wire: mapped summaries only — body/meta never included. */
+	list<R>(opts?: { map?: (e: EntrySummary<TData<TSchema>>) => R }): Promise<R[]>;
+	/** Prerender helper: `export const entries = () => col.ids().then(...)`. */
+	ids(): Promise<string[]>;
+};
+
+export function content<TDocument, TSchema extends StandardSchemaV1 | undefined = undefined>(
+	options: ContentOptions<TDocument, TSchema>
+): Collection<TDocument, TSchema> {
+	type Data = TData<TSchema>;
+
+	/** Validate + filter, then hand the survivors to the untouched engine. */
+	const buildInputs = (
+		loaded: LoadedDocument<TDocument>[]
+	): { inputs: DocsContentInput<TDocument>[]; diagnostics: DocsContentDiagnostic[] } => {
+		const inputs: DocsContentInput<TDocument>[] = [];
+		const diagnostics: DocsContentDiagnostic[] = [];
+
+		for (const doc of loaded) {
+			// 4a. schema validation → typed data or a diagnostic.
+			let data = doc.data as Data;
+			if (options.schema) {
... (322 lines total)
]]></tool_body>

Draft written. Here&#39;s the before/after and the design decisions baked into it.

## What the host writes — before vs. after

**Today** (`createAcrollsDocsSource`, three globs + a config object, all hand-keyed):
<code_block language="ts"><![CDATA[
const modules  = import.meta.glob('../content/**/*.md');
const metadata = import.meta.glob('../content/**/*.md', { eager: true, import: 'metadata' });
const facts    = import.meta.glob('../content/**/*.md', { eager: true, import: '__acrollsDocument' });
export const source = createAcrollsDocsSource({ modules, metadata, facts, contentRoot: '../content', config });
]]></code_block>
**After** (`content()`, one loader, typed frontmatter, one filter):
<code_block language="ts"><![CDATA[
export const docs = content({
  loader: markdownGlob({
    body:    import.meta.glob('../content/**/*.md'),
    modules: import.meta.glob('../content/**/*.md', { eager: true }),
    root: '../content',
  }),
  schema: v.object({ title: v.string(), section: v.string(), draft: v.optional(v.boolean()) }),
  filter: (e) => !e.data.draft,          // e.data is typed from schema
  config,                                 // your existing IA config, untouched
});
]]></code_block>
## The five design decisions in the draft

1. **It&#39;s a façade, not a rewrite.** `createDocsContentSource` — all 1000 lines of nav/breadcrumb/pager/route/admission logic — is imported and reused verbatim. `content()` only validates+filters, then hands survivors to the engine and merges diagnostics. Low blast radius.

2. **Standard Schema, not a Valibot dependency.** Types against the `~standard` interface so the host brings Valibot/Zod/Arktype. Acrolls stays dependency-neutral. Schema failure becomes an `ACROLLS_SCHEMA_INVALID` diagnostic that plugs into your existing `authored` (reject) vs `migration` (keep + warn) modes — no new failure model.

3. **`filter` ≠ `hidden`.** The draft applies `filter` by *dropping inputs before the engine sees them* → uniform removal from nav, routes, `get`, `ids`, static entries (matches Ogygia&#39;s &#34;filters narrow everywhere, including direct URL&#34;). Your existing `hidden` keeps its distinct meaning (unlisted-but-routeable, behavior 15). Two different tools, both preserved.

4. **The loader seam opens the deferred CMS/remote path at zero cost now.** `markdownGlob()` is the eager default; `customSource({ list, live })` is the interface a REST/CMS source implements later. The engine consumes `list()` output, not globs, so remote content needs no nav/route changes. `live?()` is stubbed in the type for a future SSE path without committing to it.

5. **Honest Vite limit, stated in the code.** I could *not* get to a single glob: keeping bodies lazy requires one lazy glob (`default`) + one eager glob (frontmatter). But I collapsed **three globs → two** by reading both `metadata` and `__acrollsDocument` off one eager module. The comment says so rather than pretending.

The migration shim re-expresses the old `createAcrollsDocsSource` over the *same* `content()` path (eager, sync, byte-identical behavior), so old and new hosts share one engine and you can deprecate at leisure.

## Two open questions for you before this is committable

- **`id` identity**: I used `id = doc.slug` (public/routeable, matches Ogygia ergonomics). The engine internally also has `key` (filesystem) and does alias lookups on both. If you&#39;d rather `id = key` for rename-stability, it&#39;s a one-line change but it ripples into `get()`/`ids()` semantics — worth deciding now.
- **Sync vs. async**: I exposed both `sourceSync()` (eager only, keeps the shim synchronous) and `source()` (async, uniform, required for remote loaders). Fine for a `+layout.ts` load, but if any current consumer builds nav at module top-level *synchronously*, the async remote path will force a small refactor there. Worth a grep of the example host before you lock the surface.

If you want, next steps I could take:

<message role="assistant" ordinal="31" timestamp="2026-08-14T15:45:22.289Z" has_thinking="true">
<thinking><![CDATA[
The user wants to know the actual concrete benefits of this rework for someone using acrolls, not vague praise. I should tie each change to a real, tangible payoff and be honest about which ones are genuine wins versus marginal improvements, keeping the answer tight and specific.
]]></thinking>
Fair question — let me separate real, tangible wins from cosmetics. &#34;The user&#34; here = the developer wiring acrolls into their SvelteKit site.

## Concrete benefits

**1. Less boilerplate that&#39;s easy to get wrong.**
Today the host writes three `import.meta.glob` calls that must use the *identical* pattern string and be keyed in lockstep. Miss one, or let the three patterns drift, and metadata silently detaches from bodies — a confusing failure. The rework drops it to one loader declaration. Fewer moving parts the developer can misalign.

**2. Typed frontmatter — the biggest actual win.**
Right now frontmatter is `Record&lt;string, unknown&gt;`. A typo like `titel:` or a missing `section:` surfaces as a broken page at runtime, or not at all. With `schema:`, the developer gets:
- An editor autocomplete + type error the moment `e.data.section` doesn&#39;t exist.
- A build-time diagnostic (`ACROLLS_SCHEMA_INVALID`, file + field) instead of a mystery.
This is the difference between &#34;my sidebar is empty and I don&#39;t know why&#34; and &#34;line 3 of intro.md: title is required.&#34;

**3. One visibility rule instead of scattered flags.**
`filter: (e) =&gt; !e.data.draft` is one line that reliably hides drafts from nav, breadcrumbs, pager, *and* direct-URL access. Today draft-hiding is per-doc `hidden` metadata that the developer has to remember to set on every file, and `hidden` still leaves the page routeable — so a &#34;draft&#34; is one shared link away from being public. The filter closes that gap.

**4. A door to non-file content that doesn&#39;t exist today.**
Acrolls is glob-only; PRODUCT.md explicitly *defers* remote content. With the loader seam, a developer who later wants docs from a CMS, a database, or an API writes one `customSource({ list })` and keeps their entire nav/routing/breadcrumb setup unchanged. Today that&#39;s a &#34;wait for a future acrolls feature.&#34; This makes it a thing they can do themselves.

## Honest caveats

- **Benefits 1 and 4 are latent** — they pay off at setup time and &#34;the day you outgrow files,&#34; not every day. A developer with 5 stable markdown files barely notices.
- **Benefit 2 is the everyday one.** If I had to justify the whole rework on a single point, it&#39;s typed frontmatter with build-time errors.
- **It&#39;s ergonomics, not new reader-facing capability.** None of this changes what the *published site&#39;s visitors* see — pages render identically. The audience is the developer, not their readers. If the goal were reader-facing improvement (faster pages, better search), this isn&#39;t it — that&#39;d be the CSS-code-splitting or islands items from my first message, which are separate.

So: is it worth it? Yes if acrolls expects hosts with large or evolving corpora, or wants the CMS story. Marginal if the target user is &#34;a dev with a dozen markdown files who&#39;s already wired up.&#34; The schema piece I&#39;d argue for regardless; the loader/filter refactor is a bet on acrolls&#39; users growing into it.
</message>

=== sample ===
# Plan — Authored frontmatter contract

## Implementation order

1. Add compile-time document facts to the mdsvex pipeline: whether YAML frontmatter exists,
   the initial Markdown H1 text, and authored Markdown destinations. In authored docs mode,
   strip only that initial H1.
2. Add an opt-in authored convention to the pure docs content source. It admits only valid
   Markdown documents, derives index titles from host groups, exposes diagnostics, and removes
   rejected documents from routes, entries, navigation, and pager inputs.
3. Extend the SvelteKit glob adapter to pass the generated document facts through to the content
   source. It remains lazy for article components and eager only for document facts.
4. Add reusable `DocsPageHeader` chrome, then configure the kit consumer for authored mode and
   render the title/optional description above each admitted article.
5. Extend `acrolls validate` to apply the same frontmatter and author-link rules to a corpus.
   The command is the production/CI gate; invalid input never silently becomes a route.
6. Add focused unit/CLI fixtures, package checks, a production build, and browser verification.

## Risks and controls

- Vite imports only named module exports from compiled Markdown. A stable Acrolls-owned named
  export carries compile-time document facts without executing article components.
- Runtime source construction can safely exclude metadata-invalid documents, but cannot protect
  an unrestricted Vite glob from a compile-invalid module. The CLI remains the build/CI gate for
  full-corpus compile failures.
- Author-link parsing is intentionally limited to ordinary Markdown links resolving to this
  Acrolls document source. External URLs and host-owned routes are not interpreted.
- The feature is opt-in through `convention: { mode: 'authored' }`; existing migration sources
  preserve their fallback-title behavior.

## Verification checkpoints

1. mdsvex tests prove facts are exported and only an initial H1 is removed.
2. docs tests prove admission, index-title, visibility, route, and link behavior.
3. CLI tests prove aggregate diagnostics and authored non-zero exits.
4. kit-consumer check/build prove integration; browser QA proves one visible H1 and the header.
]]></tool_result>
</message>
