<tool_body><![CDATA[
You are fixing a real type defect in the Acrolls monorepo at `/Users/amrit/fractalmandala/acrolls`, plus one stale file. You own **`.ts` files only** — another agent is concurrently editing `.md` files. Do NOT edit any `.md`.

## Background

A `content({ loader, schema, filter, config })` collection API was just added. Read `/Users/amrit/fractalmandala/acrolls/tasks/content-collection-api-tasks.md` for the full design context (especially "Fixed contract decisions", T1, and T4).

## DEFECT 1 (primary) — `StandardSchemaV1` rejects every real schema library

`packages/docs/src/lib/collection.ts` (~line 20) declares the Standard Schema `validate` member as returning ONLY the synchronous result union. The actual Standard Schema spec (`@standard-schema/spec`) declares it as `Result<Output> | Promise<Result<Output>>`.

Consequence: no spec-compliant vendor schema (Valibot, Zod, Arktype) is structurally assignable to Acrolls' `StandardSchemaV1`, so the documented `schema` option cannot be used without an `as unknown as StandardSchemaV1<...>` cast. Two separate agents hit this independently. The exact error observed:

```
Type 'ObjectSchema<...>' is not assignable to type 'StandardSchemaV1<unknown, unknown>'.
  Type 'Promise<StandardResult<...>>' is not assignable to '{ readonly issues: ... } | { readonly value: unknown; ... }'
```

This defeats fixed contract decision 4 ("Schema is Standard Schema, not a Valibot dependency"), because in practice a host cannot pass a real schema at all.

### Required fix

Widen the `validate` return type in `packages/docs/src/lib/collection.ts` to include the `Promise<...>` branch so real vendor schemas are assignable.

**Then handle the runtime consequence honestly.** Acrolls' pipeline is synchronous on the `sourceSync()` path. Decide and implement ONE of these, and justify your choice in your report:
- (a) Accept the widened type, and at runtime detect a returned Promise and throw a clear, actionable `DocsContentError`-style error naming the offending file and telling the host that async schema validation is unsupported on the synchronous path (and to use `await source()` if/when that can support it). Async validators are rare; sync validation is what Valibot/Zod do by default.
- (b) Make the async path genuinely work in `source()` (async) while `sourceSync()` throws on a Promise result.

Prefer (b) if it is clean, since `source()` is already async; fall back to (a) if (b) turns out to require restructuring. Do NOT silently ignore a Promise result or coerce it — a silently unvalidated document is the worst outcome.

Add tests in `packages/docs/src/lib/collection.test.ts` covering: a sync validator still works; a Promise-returning validator behaves per your chosen design (throws clearly on `sourceSync()`, and/or validates correctly via `source()`).

### Then remove the workaround casts

`examples/kit-consumer/src/lib/docs/source.ts` currently contains a documented `as unknown as StandardSchemaV1<unknown, v.InferOutput<...>>` cast (~lines 21-28) added purely to work around this defect. Once the type is widened, DELETE the cast and its explanatory comment so the example shows the clean intended form. Verify inference still works (a nonexistent field on `entry.data` must still be a compile error — prove this with a temporary probe, then remove the probe).

## DEFECT 2 (secondary) — stale copy-paste snippet

`docs/snippets/docs-source.ts` still contains the superseded `createDocsContentSource` + old-glob + `contentPrefix` form. `docs/README.md` advertises `docs/snippets/` as the copy-paste source of truth, so it currently ships the wrong pattern as recommended.

Rewrite it to the current form:

```ts
import { content, markdownGlob } from 'acrolls/content';
import { defineDocsConfig } from 'acrolls/docs/content';

export const docs = content({
  loader: markdownGlob({
    body: import.meta.glob('../../content/**/*.md', { import: 'default' }),
    modules: import.meta.glob('../../content/**/*.md', { eager: true }),
    root: '../../content'
  }),
  config: defineDocsConfig({ /* ... */ })
}).sourceSync();
```

A near-final reference version exists at `docs/getting-started.md` lines ~198-221 — read it and keep the snippet consistent with it. Match whatever typing/casts that reference uses.

## CONSTRAINTS

- `.ts` files ONLY. Another agent owns all `.md` and `llms.txt`. Do not edit them even if you see something wrong — report it instead.
- Do NOT modify `packages/docs/src/lib/content.ts`, `nav.ts`, `nav-path.ts`, `types.ts` (the engine stays untouched — verify with `git diff --stat`).
- Do NOT modify `packages/cli/**`.
- Do NOT add any validation library to any `packages/*` manifest. `valibot` stays in the example's devDependencies only.
- Keep `createAcrollsDocsSource`'s exported signature and error message strings unchanged.

## VERIFICATION (all must pass)

1. `cd /Users/amrit/fractalmandala/acrolls && pnpm --filter @acrolls/docs test`
2. `cd /Users/amrit/fractalmandala/acrolls && pnpm --filter @acrolls/docs check`
3. `cd /Users/amrit/fractalmandala/acrolls && pnpm --filter @acrolls/docs build`
4. `cd /Users/amrit/fractalmandala/acrolls && pnpm --filter @acrolls/sveltekit check && pnpm --filter @acrolls/sveltekit build`
5. `cd /Users/amrit/fractalmandala/acrolls && pnpm --filter @acrolls/example-kit check` — must pass with the cast REMOVED from source.ts
6. `cd /Users/amrit/fractalmandala/acrolls && pnpm --filter @acrolls/example-kit build`
7. `git diff --stat packages/docs/src/lib/content.ts` — empty
8. Prove a real Valibot schema is now assignable with NO cast (the example host itself is the proof).

RETURN: (a) files edited, (b) the exact before/after of the `validate` type declaration, (c) which runtime design you chose ((a) or (b) above) and why, (d) the new tests added, (e) proof that inference still catches a nonexistent field, (f) confirmation the example cast is gone and the host typechecks, (g) the final content of `docs/snippets/docs-source.ts`, (h) pass/fail of all 8 verification steps, (i) anything you found that the `.md` agent needs to know.
]]></tool_body>
<tool_result><![CDATA[
All 8 verification steps pass. Report follows.

## (a) Files edited
- `/Users/amrit/fractalmandala/acrolls/packages/docs/src/lib/collection.ts`
- `/Users/amrit/fractalmandala/acrolls/packages/docs/src/lib/collection.test.ts`
- `/Users/amrit/fractalmandala/acrolls/packages/docs/src/lib/index.ts` (re-export two new public types)
- `/Users/amrit/fractalmandala/acrolls/examples/kit-consumer/src/lib/docs/source.ts`
- `/Users/amrit/fractalmandala/acrolls/docs/snippets/docs-source.ts`

No `.md`, no `llms.txt`, no manifests, no `packages/cli/**`, no engine files touched.

## (b) The `validate` declaration, before → after

Before:
```ts
readonly validate: (
    value: unknown
) =>
    | { readonly value: Output; readonly issues?: undefined }
    | { readonly issues: ReadonlyArray<{ readonly message: string;
          readonly path?: ReadonlyArray<PropertyKey | { key: PropertyKey }>; }>; };
```

After (the two arms are now named types, both exported):
```ts
export type StandardSchemaIssue = {
    readonly message: string;
    readonly path?: ReadonlyArray<PropertyKey | { key: PropertyKey }>;
};
export type StandardSchemaResult<Output> =
    | { readonly value: Output; readonly issues?: undefined }
    | { readonly issues: ReadonlyArray<StandardSchemaIssue> };

readonly validate: (
    value: unknown
) => StandardSchemaResult<Output> | Promise<StandardSchemaResult<Output>>;
```

## (c) Runtime design: option (b), the async path genuinely works
`source()` was already `async`, and the only obstacle was that a single `runPipeline` served both entry points. Splitting it cost no restructuring: `runPipelineAsync` awaits each `validate` result, `runPipelineSync` calls `validate` and throws if the return value is thenable, and both delegate everything after validation (diagnostic, authored-vs-migration admission, `filter`, engine-input push) to one shared `admit()` helper — so the two paths cannot drift. `sourceSync()` throws a `DocsContentError`:

> `Schema validation for "guides/a.md" returned a Promise. Asynchronous schema validation is not supported on the synchronous path — use "await source()" instead of "sourceSync()", or supply a synchronous validator.`

Because `get()`, `list()`, and `ids()` all route through `source()`, an async validator works end-to-end on every surface except the deliberately synchronous one. Nothing is ever silently unvalidated. `DocsContentError` is imported from `content.js` (import only — the engine file is byte-identical).

## (d) New tests (3, all in `collection.test.ts`)
1. **`validates through an async Standard Schema on source() and throws on sourceSync()`** — an `async validate` wrapper over the existing stub; asserts via `source()` that schema defaults reach the engine (`{title:'B', order:0}`, nav order `b, bad, a`) and that the `ACROLLS_SCHEMA_INVALID` diagnostic is emitted verbatim; then asserts `sourceSync()` throws `DocsContentError` matching `/guides\/a\.md/` and `/await source\(\)/`.
2. **`drops async schema failures in authored mode via source()`** — a `Promise.resolve(...)`-returning validator; proves authored-mode admission still drops the invalid document on the async path.
3. **`keeps a synchronous schema fully synchronous on sourceSync()`** — counts validator invocations to prove the sync path still runs inline and validated output reaches metadata.

48/48 tests pass.

## (e) Inference proof
Temporarily changed the example's filter to `!entry.data.nonexistentField`; `pnpm --filter @acrolls/example-kit check` failed with:

> `src/lib/docs/source.ts 31:33 "Property 'nonexistentField' does not exist on type '{ title?: string | undefined; description?: ...; order?: number | undefined; hidden?: boolean | undefined; draft?: boolean | undefined; }'."`

That type is the Valibot `InferOutput`, reached with no cast anywhere. Probe reverted to `!entry.data.draft`; check is back to 0 errors.

## (f) Cast removed
The `as unknown as StandardSchemaV1<...>` line, its 6-line explanatory comment, the now-dead `schema` alias, and the `type StandardSchemaV1` import are all gone. The example now reads `schema: frontmatter` — the raw Valibot `v.object(...)` passed directly. Host typechecks and builds clean.

## (g) `docs/snippets/docs-source.ts` — final content
Rewritten to match `docs/getting-started.md` ~198-221 exactly, including its `as Record<string, () => Promise<DocsArticle>>` cast on the lazy glob (the reference version's only cast) and its `../../docs` paths:

```ts
import type { Component } from 'svelte';
import { content, markdownGlob } from 'acrolls/content';
import { defineDocsConfig } from 'acrolls/docs/content';

type DocsArticle = Component;

export const docs = content({
	loader: markdownGlob<DocsArticle>({
		body: import.meta.glob('../../docs/**/*.md', { import: 'default' }) as Record<
			string,
			() => Promise<DocsArticle>
		>,
		modules: import.meta.glob('../../docs/**/*.md', { eager: true }),
		root: '../../docs'
	}),
	config: defineDocsConfig({
		title: 'Documentation',
		baseHref: '/docs',
		subtitle: 'Generated from Markdown'
	})
}).sourceSync();
```

## (h) Verification: 8/8 pass
1. `@acrolls/docs test` — PASS (48 tests, 3 files)
2. `@acrolls/docs check` — PASS (306 files, 0 errors)
3. `@acrolls/docs build` — PASS (`dist/collection.js` + `dist/collection.d.ts` emitted)
4. `@acrolls/sveltekit check && build` — PASS
5. `@acrolls/example-kit check` with the cast removed — PASS (421 files, 0 errors)
6. `@acrolls/example-kit build` — PASS
7. `git diff --stat packages/docs/src/lib/content.ts` — empty (also verified empty for `nav.ts`, `nav-path.ts`, `types.ts`, and `packages/cli` shows only the pre-existing `onboarding.ts` changes I did not make)
8. Real Valibot schema assignable with no cast — proven by step 5 plus the (e) probe

## (i) For the `.md` agent
- **The workaround is documented in prose somewhere.** The cast's comment claimed Acrolls "currently declares only the synchronous branch" — that is now false. If any `.md` (likely `docs/integrate-sveltekit.md#typed-frontmatter-with-schema`, which `getting-started.md` points at for the `schema` option) shows or mentions an `as unknown as StandardSchemaV1<...>` cast, delete it: hosts now pass `schema: frontmatter` directly.
- **New documentable contract worth a sentence:** `StandardSchemaV1.validate` is `Result | Promise<Result>`. Async validators validate correctly through `await source()` (and therefore `get()`/`list()`/`ids()`); `sourceSync()` throws a `DocsContentError` naming the offending file. Valibot and Zod object schemas are synchronous, so the common case is unaffected.
- **Two new exported type names** are now public and may belong in any API-surface table: `StandardSchemaIssue` and `StandardSchemaResult<Output>`, exported from `packages/docs/src/lib/index.ts` alongside `StandardSchemaV1`.

<tool_body><![CDATA[
You are correcting a factually wrong, security-adjacent claim in the Acrolls documentation at `/Users/amrit/fractalmandala/acrolls`. You own **`.md` files and `llms.txt` only** — another agent is concurrently editing `.ts` files. Do NOT edit any `.ts`, `.js`, `.svelte`, or `.json`.

## Background

A `content({ loader, schema, filter, config })` collection API was just added, with a `filter` option that decides which documents are published. Read `/Users/amrit/fractalmandala/acrolls/tasks/content-collection-api-tasks.md` for design context.

Documentation was just written across many `.md` files claiming that a `filter`-removed document has **"no presence in the client bundle"** and is excluded from build output.

## THE DEFECT: that claim is false, and it is dangerous

Empirically verified in the example host: a `draft: true` page removed by `filter` still has its compiled body present in BOTH:
- `.svelte-kit/output/server/chunks/source.js`
- `.svelte-kit/output/client/_app/immutable/chunks/*.js`

**Root cause:** `import.meta.glob` materializes every matching file into the module graph at build time, and `filter` runs at runtime afterward. Rollup cannot tree-shake it away. This is inherent to glob-based loading, not a bug in the filter.

**What IS true:** a filtered document has no route, no navigation entry, no breadcrumb/pager entry, is not prerendered as a page, and a direct URL visit 404s. It is genuinely unroutable.

**What is NOT true:** that its content is absent from shipped bytes.

**Why this matters:** a reader could reasonably conclude `filter: (e) => !e.data.draft` makes unpublished drafts confidential. It does not. Someone could ship embargoed content believing it is not on the wire. This must be stated plainly, not buried.

## Your task

Find and correct EVERY place this overstatement appears. Known locations (there may be more — grep thoroughly for phrases like "client bundle", "not in the bundle", "prerendered", "removed from every surface"):

1. **`PRODUCT.md` behavior 69** — currently reads in part: "it has no route, no navigation entry, no prerendered output, and no presence in the client bundle." The bundle clause must go, replaced with an accurate statement plus an explicit note that `filter` is a publication/routing boundary, NOT a confidentiality or access-control boundary, and that with a glob loader the document's compiled body may still be present in build output.
2. **`docs/integrate-sveltekit.md`** — the "`filter` versus `hidden`" comparison table and surrounding prose. The table has a row/claim about the client bundle.
3. Any condensed restatements in: `docs/content-authoring.md`, `packages/sveltekit/README.md`, `packages/docs/README.md`, `docs/checklist.md`, `llms.txt`, `TECH.md`.

Also correct the source spec so it doesn't perpetuate the error:
4. **`tasks/content-collection-api-tasks.md`** — T7 step 8 currently says filtered documents "must not appear in prerendered routes, the nav JSON, or the client bundle". Reword the bundle clause to match reality, and note the glob-materialization reason.

## Required framing

Be direct and useful, not alarmist. The accurate statement is roughly:

> `filter` removes a document from every *addressable* surface: no route, no nav entry, no breadcrumb or pager entry, not prerendered, and a direct URL 404s. It is a publication boundary, not a confidentiality boundary. With the Markdown glob loader, Vite materializes every file matching the glob into the module graph, so a filtered document's compiled body can still be present in build output even though nothing routes to it. Do not use `filter` to protect secret or embargoed content — keep that content out of the globbed directory entirely, or behind host-owned authentication.

Add the "keep it out of the globbed directory" guidance wherever you explain drafts, since that is the actual remedy.

Note this limitation is specific to glob loaders — a `customSource` that simply never returns the document does not materialize it at all. Mention that where relevant.

## CONSTRAINTS

- `.md` and `llms.txt` ONLY. Another agent owns `.ts` files (including `docs/snippets/docs-source.ts` — do not touch it).
- Do NOT renumber existing PRODUCT.md behaviors. Edit behavior 69's text in place.
- Keep `hidden`'s documented semantics unchanged and still clearly distinguished from `filter` — the distinction remains real and valuable, only the bundle claim was wrong.
- Do NOT introduce performance or rendering claims anywhere.
- Match each file's existing voice and formatting.
- Another agent may concurrently remove an `as unknown as StandardSchemaV1<...>` cast from the example host by widening a type. If any `.md` you touch documents or explains that cast as a necessary workaround, remove that explanation — assume the cast is gone and schemas work directly. Report it if you find such text.

## VERIFICATION

1. `grep -rni "client bundle" --include="*.md" --include="*.txt" . | grep -v node_modules` — every remaining hit must be accurate.
2. `grep -rn "filter" --include="*.md" PRODUCT.md docs/ packages/*/README.md llms.txt | grep -v node_modules` — review each for the overstatement.
3. Re-read your edited behavior 69 and the comparison table end-to-end for internal consistency.

RETURN: (a) every file edited and the specific claim corrected in each, (b) the full final text of PRODUCT.md behavior 69, (c) the full final text of the `filter` vs `hidden` table/section in `docs/integrate-sveltekit.md`, (d) the reworded T7 step 8, (e) grep results proving no inaccurate bundle claim remains, (f) any additional overstatement you found beyond the bundle claim, (g) whether you found and removed any text explaining the schema cast workaround.
]]></tool_body>
<tool_result><![CDATA[
Done. All greps now clean.

## (a) Files edited and claim corrected

| File | Correction |
|---|---|
| `/Users/amrit/fractalmandala/acrolls/PRODUCT.md` | Behavior 69: dropped "no presence in the client bundle"; added publication-vs-confidentiality framing + glob materialization + `customSource` note. Not renumbered. |
| `/Users/amrit/fractalmandala/acrolls/docs/integrate-sveltekit.md` | `filter` vs `hidden` table + prose: removed bundle claim, added an accurate "Compiled body in build output" row and a new boundary paragraph. Also removed the schema cast (see g). |
| `/Users/amrit/fractalmandala/acrolls/docs/content-authoring.md` | Draft section: removed "and the client bundle"; added boundary paragraph + "keep it out of the globbed directory" remedy. Line 55 "every surface" → "every addressable surface". |
| `/Users/amrit/fractalmandala/acrolls/docs/getting-started.md` | "removes documents from the surface entirely" → addressable-surface wording + boundary caveat. |
| `/Users/amrit/fractalmandala/acrolls/docs/checklist.md` | Added a checklist item: secret/embargoed content kept out of the globbed directory. |
| `/Users/amrit/fractalmandala/acrolls/packages/sveltekit/README.md` | "every surface" → "every addressable surface" + boundary sentence. |
| `/Users/amrit/fractalmandala/acrolls/packages/docs/README.md` | Two spots (lines ~45 and ~76) corrected; added the build-output caveat. |
| `/Users/amrit/fractalmandala/acrolls/llms.txt` | Corrected the `filter` bullet, added a dedicated boundary bullet. |
| `/Users/amrit/fractalmandala/acrolls/TECH.md` | Pipeline prose: added the `import.meta.glob` / no-tree-shake explanation. |
| `/Users/amrit/fractalmandala/acrolls/tasks/content-collection-api-tasks.md` | T7 step 8 reworded; also T6 contract item 3 (line ~462) which restated the same overstatement. |
| `/Users/amrit/fractalmandala/acrolls/examples/kit-consumer/src/content/guides/draft-note.md` | The example draft itself claimed removal "from every surface" and "filtered pages do not exist" — corrected (this is the file empirically found in both chunks). |

## (b) PRODUCT.md behavior 69, final

> 69. A collection accepts one uniform filter that decides publication. A filtered document is removed from every addressable surface: it has no route, no navigation entry, no breadcrumb or pager entry, no prerendered output, and a direct URL visit 404s. This is distinct from `hidden` (behavior 15), which remains unlisted-but-routeable and still prerendered. A host that wants a document unreachable uses the filter; a host that wants it unlinked but reachable uses `hidden`. The two compose independently. `filter` is a publication and routing boundary, not a confidentiality or access-control boundary: with the Markdown glob loader, Vite materializes every file matching the glob into the module graph before the filter runs, so a filtered document's compiled body can still be present in build output even though nothing routes to it. Secret or embargoed content belongs outside the globbed directory, or behind host-owned authentication. A `customSource` that never returns the document does not materialize it at all.

## (c) `filter` vs `hidden` section, final

Table rows unchanged except one added row; `hidden` semantics untouched.

```
| | `filter` (collection option) | `hidden: true` (frontmatter / folder or entry config) |
|---|---|---|
| Where it lives | `content({ filter })` | Document frontmatter or `defineDocsConfig` |
| Sidebar / `DocsNav` | Absent | Absent |
| `docs.get()` / `docs.load()` | Absent | **Resolves normally** |
| `entries()` / `ids()` / prerender | Absent | **Present — the page is still built** |
| Direct URL visit | 404 | **Renders the page** |
| Breadcrumbs and pager | Absent | Absent from listings, reachable by URL |
| Compiled body in build output | **May still be present** (glob loader) | Present |
```

**`filter` removes a document from every addressable surface, including direct URL access.** It runs before the route engine, so a filtered document never becomes a route at all — it is not in the nav JSON and not in the prerendered output. Use it for drafts, for content gated by environment, and for anything that must not be reachable by guessing a URL.

**`filter` is a publication boundary, not a confidentiality boundary.** With the Markdown glob loader, Vite materializes every file matching the glob into the module graph at build time and `filter` runs afterwards, so Rollup cannot tree-shake the document away: a filtered document's compiled body can still be present in the built server and client chunks even though nothing routes to it. Do not use `filter` to protect secret or embargoed content. Keep that content out of the globbed directory entirely, or put it behind host-owned authentication. This limitation is specific to glob loaders — a `customSource({ list })` that simply never returns the document does not materialize it at all.

**`hidden` means unlisted, not private.** (unchanged) — then the unchanged `{ key, data, meta }` / no-`id` paragraph.

## (d) T7 step 8, reworded

> 8. Confirm `filter`-removed documents are absent from every addressable surface: they must not appear in prerendered routes or the nav JSON, and a direct URL visit must 404. Do **not** assert they are absent from the built chunks — `import.meta.glob` materializes every matching file into the module graph at build time and `filter` runs afterwards, so Rollup cannot tree-shake a filtered document's compiled body out of the server or client output. That is inherent to glob-based loading, not a bug in the filter. Verify instead that nothing routes to the document, and that the docs state plainly that `filter` is a publication boundary rather than a confidentiality boundary.

## (e) Verification greps

`grep -rni "client bundle" --include="*.md" --include="*.txt" .` (minus node_modules) → **zero hits**. `grep -rni "from every surface\|removed everywhere\|no presence in"` → **zero hits**. Every remaining "build output" hit is one of the new accurate statements (or unrelated: `tasks/styles-entrypoints-todo.md`, `docs/cli.md`).

## (f) Additional overstatements found beyond the bundle claim

Three beyond the assigned list, all now fixed:
- `docs/getting-started.md:231` — "`filter` removes documents from the surface entirely," a vaguer version of the same overreach, in the file most new users read first.
- `packages/docs/README.md:45` — a second, separate "every surface including direct URL access" restatement in addition to the line-74 one named in the task.
- `examples/kit-consumer/src/content/guides/draft-note.md` — its frontmatter description and body asserted "filtered pages do not exist." This was the most acute instance: it is the actual file whose compiled body was verified present in both chunks, so the page was a live counterexample to its own text. It now says so explicitly and self-referentially.

Note `docs/frontmatter-contract-spec.md:100-105` was already accurate and independently corroborates the root cause ("An unrestricted `import.meta.glob()` can still let Vite reach a broken module before a runtime filter runs"), declining to claim runtime exclusion. Left unchanged — the corrected docs are now consistent with it, where before they contradicted it.

## (g) Schema cast workaround text

Yes — found and removed, in `docs/integrate-sveltekit.md`. It was a three-line comment plus a `const schema = frontmatter as unknown as StandardSchemaV1<unknown, v.InferOutput<typeof frontmatter>>` line, explaining that Acrolls declares only the synchronous `~standard.validate` branch and that the cast "can be dropped once Acrolls widens the declared type." The example now declares `const schema = v.object({...})` and passes it directly; the now-unused `type StandardSchemaV1` was dropped from that import. `docs/packages.md` still lists `StandardSchemaV1` as an exported type, which remains correct and is not a workaround explanation, so it was left alone. No other `.md` referenced the cast. Note this edit assumes the concurrent agent's type widening lands — if it does not, this sample will not typecheck against the shipped types.

Constraints respected: no `.ts`/`.js`/`.svelte`/`.json` touched, no behaviors renumbered, `hidden` semantics unchanged throughout, and no performance or rendering claims introduced.agentId: a096ddd9e9c64eee8 (use SendMessage with to: 'a096ddd9e9c64eee8', summary: '<5-10 word recap>' to continue this agent)

<message role="assistant" ordinal="69" timestamp="2026-08-14T18:58:27.678Z">
Spot-checks confirm every load-bearing claim. All seven tasks are complete and verified.

## What shipped

| Wave | Tasks | Agents |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | T1 core `content()` façade | 1 |
| 2 | T2 loaders + T3 public export | 1 |
| 3 | T4 example ‖ T5 CLI ‖ T6 docs | 3 parallel |
| 4 | Corrective: schema type ‖ doc accuracy | 2 parallel |
| 5 | T7 verification | 1 |

I used parallel dispatch only where the skill&#39;s criteria actually held. T1→T2→T3 is a hard dependency chain, so fanning those out would have produced garbage. T4/T5/T6 (example / CLI / docs) had zero file overlap and ran concurrently; the corrective wave was split by file *type* (`.ts` vs `.md`) so two agents couldn&#39;t collide on the same fix.

## Two real defects the parallel dispatch caught

Fanning out is what surfaced these — two agents hit the same wall independently, which is stronger evidence than either alone:

**1. The headline feature didn&#39;t work.** `StandardSchemaV1.validate` declared only the sync branch, but the spec is `Result | Promise&lt;Result&gt;`. No Valibot/Zod/Arktype schema was structurally assignable, so both T4 and T6 had to paper over it with `as unknown as` casts. Fixed properly: async validators now work through `source()`, and `sourceSync()` throws a named error instead of silently skipping validation. The example now passes a raw `v.object(...)` — `schema: frontmatter`, no cast.

**2. A security-adjacent false claim had already been written into PRODUCT.md.** T6 documented that `filter` leaves &#34;no presence in the client bundle.&#34; T4 empirically disproved it — `import.meta.glob` materializes every match before the filter runs, so Rollup can&#39;t tree-shake filtered bodies out. Someone could have shipped embargoed drafts believing they were off the wire. `filter` is now documented as a publication boundary, not a confidentiality one, with the actual remedy (keep it out of the globbed directory). The `grep` for &#34;client bundle&#34; now returns zero hits — removed, not softened.

The draft fixture itself had claimed &#34;filtered pages do not exist&#34; — a file that was a live counterexample to its own text. Also fixed.

## Verification

- `pnpm install &amp;&amp; pnpm build` clean across all 7 packages; **88 tests pass**, `pnpm -r check` 0 errors/0 warnings.
- **Backward compat:** the pre-migration three-glob `createAcrollsDocsSource` host, restored verbatim from `git show HEAD:`, still builds and serves with no source changes and no new warnings.
- **Nav parity:** old vs. new `docs.nav` over an identical content set is **byte-identical** (same md5). The only divergence anywhere is the one document the host deliberately filtered.
- Engine files (`content.ts`, `nav.ts`, `nav-path.ts`, `types.ts`) provably untouched — the façade claim holds.
- No validation library leaked into any shipped manifest; `valibot` is example-only devDependency.

Two honest caveats from T7, neither a defect: `acrolls studio` was exercised non-interactively via `--no-open` + HTTP fetch rather than a visible browser, and the prerender check ran against the served production build because the example doesn&#39;t enable prerendering.

Nothing is committed — 26 modified files and 6 new ones are sitting in the working tree for your review. Want me to commit this as a series (one commit per task) or as a single change?
</message>

import { mdsvex } from 'mdsvex';
import adapter from '@sveltejs/adapter-vercel';

=== release.md ===
---
title: Release Acrolls
description: Build, verify, publish, and test the bundled Acrolls npm package.
tags:
  - release
  - npm
  - verification
---

## Release contract

Consumers install one package:

```bash
pnpm add acrolls@0.1.3
```

The public tarball bundles its internal `@acrolls/*` runtime packages and the unist visitor
closure required by the compiled mdsvex path. Publish only `acrolls`; the scoped implementation
units do not need separate registry releases. Do not test the obsolete `acrolls@0.0.1` artifact.

## Verify from a clean checkout

Build before type-checking because workspace package exports point at generated `dist` files:

```bash
pnpm install
pnpm build
pnpm check
pnpm test
pnpm --filter @acrolls/example-kit check
pnpm --filter @acrolls/example-kit build
pnpm verify:packed-consumer
```

Create and inspect the public tarball with:

```bash
pnpm release:pack
```

The release script handles the package-layout details needed to bundle Acrolls' private
workspace packages. Confirm that runtime files, README, license, public exports, and bundled
dependencies are present and test/source files are absent.

## Publish the public package

Confirm the npm identity first:

```bash
npm whoami
```

Then publish the public package once:

```bash
pnpm release
```

If npm accepts the version, it cannot be published again. Fix any later problem, bump the
=== scripts ===
verify-packed-consumer.mjs
]]></tool_result>
</message>

import { error } from '@sveltejs/kit';
import type { EntryGenerator, PageLoad } from './$types';
import { docs } from '../../../lib/docs/source';

export const entries: EntryGenerator = () =>
	docs.documents.filter((document) => document.slug).map((document) => ({ slug: document.slug }));

export const load: PageLoad = ({ params }) => {
	const slug = params.slug ?? '';
	if (!docs.get(slug)) error(404, `Documentation page "${slug || 'index'}" not found`);
	return { slug };
};

+import { createAcrollsSvelteKitMdsvexPreprocessor } from 'acrolls/sveltekit';
+import adapter from '@sveltejs/adapter-vercel';
+import { sveltekit } from '@sveltejs/kit/vite';
+import { defineConfig } from 'vite';
+
+export default defineConfig({
+	plugins: [
+		sveltekit({
+			compilerOptions: {
+				// Force runes mode for the project, except for libraries. Can be removed in svelte 6.
+				runes: ({ filename }) =>
+					filename.split(/[/\\]/).includes('node_modules') ? undefined : true
+			},
+			adapter: adapter(),
+			// Replaces the bare mdsvex() call. Acrolls owns the Markdown pipeline now:
+			// GFM, frontmatter, heading slugs, table wrapping, Shiki code frames, and the
+			// source-safety normalization that keeps a malformed document from taking down
+			// the build. Do not stack a second mdsvex preprocessor alongside this one.
+			preprocess: [createAcrollsSvelteKitMdsvexPreprocessor()],
+			extensions: ['.svelte', '.svx', '.md']
+		})
+	]
+});
]]></tool_body>

Switching\n * `convention.mode` to 'authored' below would flip that to fail-fast.\n *\n * `title` in particular stays optional: the content source already derives a readable title\n * from the filename when frontmatter has none, and a few older files use a capitalised\n * `Title:` key that would otherwise reject them.\n */\nconst frontmatter = v.object({\n\ttitle: v.optional(v.string()),\n\tdescription: v.optional(v.string()),\n\tdate: v.optional(v.union([v.string(), v.date()])),\n\ttags: v.optional(v.array(v.string())),\n\tgroup: v.optional(v.string()),\n\tsupergroup: v.optional(v.string()),\n\torder: v.optional(v.number()),\n\thidden: v.optional(v.boolean()),\n\tdraft: v.optional(v.boolean())\n});\n\nexport const docs = content({\n\tloader: markdownGlob\u003cDocsArticle\u003e({\n\t\t// Both globs use the identical pattern. `body` stays lazy so 307 article\n\t\t// components are not pulled into the eager module graph; `modules` is eager and\n\t\t// carries frontmatter plus Acrolls' compile-time document facts.\n\t\tbody: import.meta.glob('../../docs/**/*.md', { import: 'default' }) as Record\u003c\n\t\t\tstring,\n\t\t\t() =\u003e Promise\u003cDocsArticle\u003e\n\t\t\u003e,\n\t\tmodules: import.meta.glob('../../docs/**/*.md', { eager: true }),\n\t\troot: '../../docs'\n\t}),\n\tschema: frontmatter,\n\t// Publication boundary, not a confidentiality boundary: a filtered document has no\n\t// route and 404s, but with a glob loader its compiled body can still be present in\n\t// build output. Secret content belongs outside src/docs entirely.\n\tfilter: (entry) =\u003e !entry.data.draft,\n\tconfig: defineDocsConfig({\n\t\ttitle: 'Fractal Mandala',\n\t\tbaseHref: '/docs',\n\t\tsubtitle: '307 documents · writings, civilization, history',\n\t\tfolders: {\n\t\t\twritings: { title: 'Writings', order: 1 },\n\t\t\tcivilization: { title: 'Civilization', order: 2 },\n\t\t\t'comparative-civilization': { title: 'Comparative Civilization', order: 3 },\n\t\t\thistory: { title: 'History', order: 4 },\n\t\t\t'karmic-streams': { title: 'Karmic Streams', order: 5 },\n\t\t\t'sri-aurobindo': { title: 'Sri Aurobindo', order: 6 },\n\t\t\t'ram-swarup-sita-ram-goel': { title: 'Ram Swarup \u0026 Sita Ram Goel', order: 7 }\n\t\t}\n\t})\n}).sourceSync();\n"}

<tool_body><![CDATA[
@@ -0,0 +1,65 @@
+import type { Component } from 'svelte';
+import * as v from 'valibot';
+import { content, markdownGlob } from 'acrolls/content';
+import { defineDocsConfig } from 'acrolls/docs/content';
+
+type DocsArticle = Component;
+
+/**
+ * Frontmatter contract for this corpus.
+ *
+ * Every field is optional on purpose. This is a migration-mode corpus of 307 documents
+ * written over several years, so the schema's job here is to *describe and report*, not to
+ * gate. A document whose frontmatter does not match is kept and surfaces an
+ * ACROLLS_SCHEMA_INVALID diagnostic; it is not dropped. Switching
+ * `convention.mode` to 'authored' below would flip that to fail-fast.
+ *
+ * `title` in particular stays optional: the content source already derives a readable title
+ * from the filename when frontmatter has none, and a few older files use a capitalised
+ * `Title:` key that would otherwise reject them.
+ */
+const frontmatter = v.object({
+	title: v.optional(v.string()),
+	description: v.optional(v.string()),
+	date: v.optional(v.union([v.string(), v.date()])),
+	tags: v.optional(v.array(v.string())),
+	group: v.optional(v.string()),
+	supergroup: v.optional(v.string()),
+	order: v.optional(v.number()),
+	hidden: v.optional(v.boolean()),
+	draft: v.optional(v.boolean())
+});
+
+export const docs = content({
+	loader: markdownGlob<DocsArticle>({
+		// Both globs use the identical pattern. `body` stays lazy so 307 article
+		// components are not pulled into the eager module graph; `modules` is eager and
+		// carries frontmatter plus Acrolls' compile-time document facts.
+		body: import.meta.glob('../../docs/**/*.md', { import: 'default' }) as Record<
+			string,
+			() => Promise<DocsArticle>
+		>,
+		modules: import.meta.glob('../../docs/**/*.md', { eager: true }),
+		root: '../../docs'
+	}),
+	schema: frontmatter,
+	// Publication boundary, not a confidentiality boundary: a filtered document has no
+	// route and 404s, but with a glob loader its compiled body can still be present in
+	// build output. Secret content belongs outside src/docs entirely.
+	filter: (entry) => !entry.data.draft,
+	config: defineDocsConfig({
+		title: 'Fractal Mandala',
+		baseHref: '/docs',
+		subtitle: '307 documents · writings, civilization, history',
+		folders: {
+			writings: { title: 'Writings', order: 1 },

+<script lang="ts">
+	import { DocsPageHeader } from 'acrolls/docs';
+	import { docs } from './source';
+
+	let { slug }: { slug: string } = $props();
+	const document = $derived(docs.get(slug));
+</script>
+
+<svelte:head>
+	<title>{document?.title ?? 'Docs'} · Fractal Mandala</title>
+	{#if document?.description}
+		<meta name="description" content={document.description} />
+	{/if}
+</svelte:head>
+
+{#if document}
+	{#await document.loader()}
+		<p>Loading…</p>
+	{:then Article}
+		<DocsPageHeader title={document.title} description={document.description} />
+		<Article />
+	{:catch error}
+		<DocsPageHeader title={document.title} description={document.description} />
+		<p class="docs-error">
+			This document could not be rendered: {error instanceof Error
+				? error.message
+				: String(error)}
+		</p>
+	{/await}
+{:else}
+	<p>Document not found.</p>
+{/if}
+
]]></tool_body>

<tool_body><![CDATA[
@@ -0,0 +1,63 @@
+<script lang="ts">
+	import { DocsPageHeader } from 'acrolls/docs';
+	import { docs } from '$lib/docs/source';
+
+	// This corpus has no index.md, so Acrolls does not invent a landing page or redirect
+	// the mount to an arbitrary article. The host owns this overview instead.
+	const sections = docs.nav.sections;
+	const total = docs.documents.length;
+</script>
+
+<svelte:head>
+	<title>Docs · Fractal Mandala</title>
+	<meta name="description" content="Writings, civilization, and history — {total} documents." />
+</svelte:head>
+
+<DocsPageHeader
+	title={docs.nav.title}
+	description="{total} documents across {sections.length} sections."
+/>
+
+<ul class="docs-index">
+	{#each sections as section (section.id)}
+		<li>
+			<h2>{section.title}</h2>
+			<p>{section.items.length} pages</p>
+			<ul>
+				{#each section.items.slice(0, 6) as item (item.id)}
+					<li>
+						{#if item.href}
+							<a href={item.href}>{item.title}</a>
+						{:else}
+							{item.title}
+						{/if}
+					</li>
+				{/each}
+			</ul>
+		</li>
+	{/each}
+</ul>
+
+<style>
+	.docs-index {
+		list-style: none;
+		padding: 0;
+		display: grid;
+		gap: 2rem;
+		grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fill, minmax(240px, 1fr));
+	}
+	.docs-index ul {
+		list-style: none;
+		padding: 0;
+	}
+	.docs-index h2 {
+		margin: 0 0 0.25rem;
+		font-size: 1rem;
+	}
+	.docs-index p {
+		margin: 0 0 0.5rem;
+		opacity: 0.7;
+		font-size: 0.85rem;
+	}
+</style>
+
]]></tool_body>

<thinking><![CDATA[
I've found a genuine incompatibility: mdsvex's generated layout wrapper uses `$$props`, a legacy Svelte 4 API, but the host forces runes mode on all non-node_modules files including the compiled markdown, making `$$props` illegal there.

The clean fix is host-owned: exempt `.md`/`.svx` files from the forced-runes predicate in the vite config, treating mdsvex output like a legacy-mode library artifact rather than waiting on mdsvex to add runes support. This is worth flagging as a real integration finding, tying into the PRODUCT.md note about deferring mdsvex layout/snippet support, and the host guidance should call out that runes-mode hosts need to exempt markdown extensions. Let me go update the vite config now.
]]></thinking>
A genuine incompatibility, and exactly what a test deployment exists to find: **mdsvex&#39;s generated layout wrapper emits `{...$$props}`** (legacy Svelte 4 API), but this host forces `runes: true` for everything outside `node_modules` — including the compiled `.md` files. Runes mode forbids `$$props`.

The fix is host-side: markdown-compiled components are legacy artifacts, like libraries, so they must be exempt from the runes override.

-			compilerOptions: {
-				// Force runes mode for the project, except for libraries. Can be removed in svelte 6.
-				runes: ({ filename }) =>
-					filename.split(/[/\\]/).includes('node_modules') ? undefined : true
-			},
+			compilerOptions: {
+				// Force runes mode for the project, except for libraries. Can be removed in svelte 6.
+				//
+				// Markdown is also exempt. mdsvex generates its layout wrapper as
+				// `<Layout {...$$props} {...metadata}>`, and `$$props` is a legacy API that
+				// runes mode rejects outright. Compiled .md/.svx output is a generated
+				// legacy-mode artifact like any library file, so it opts out the same way.
+				runes: ({ filename }) => {
+					if (/\.(md|svx)$/.test(filename)) return undefined;
+					return filename.split(/[/\\]/).includes('node_modules') ? undefined : true;
+				}
+			},
]]></tool_body>

I've pinpointed the root cause: the host defines a dark-mode palette where foreground is light text meant for a dark background, but acrolls' CSS inherits that light foreground variable while rendering its own white background, producing near-invisible light-on-light text.

This reveals a real gap in acrolls' host-fallback design — it consumes `--foreground`/`--background` without verifying they're consistent with the background it actually paints, so any host with a dark theme breaks readability. The fix is to explicitly scope the `--acrolls-*` variables on the docs surface so it stops inheriting the host's dark-theme tokens, and I want to check the acrolls default CSS to confirm the correct variable names before writing the override.
]]></thinking>

Now it's clear the host defines light theme variables at root and overrides them for dark mode using a selector that excludes explicit light-mode markers, meaning the browser's dark mode preference is what's driving the color scheme here.

But the body background rendered white despite dark-mode tokens being active, which means something in the host's styles is setting a hardcoded light background instead of using the theme variable. This looks like an inconsistency bug in the host's own CSS — the tokens say dark, but the actual body background isn't following them, and it only became visible because acrolls properly consumes those tokens. I want to check the host's global stylesheet to confirm the body rule before deciding on the simplest fix.
]]></thinking>
Now it&#39;s fully diagnosed. The host has a proper theme system — `:root` light, plus a `prefers-color-scheme: dark` block flipping `--foreground` to near-white. **The browser is in dark mode**, so `--foreground` flipped, but the host&#39;s body background stayed light. Acrolls faithfully consumed the flipped token and exposed the host&#39;s own theme inconsistency.

]]></arguments>

I'm finding the real issue: the host page actually uses two separate token systems, one from _tokens.sass (--bg, --text-primary) and a shadcn-style one from _colors.sass (--background, --foreground). The measured body color matches the light-mode value of --text-primary even though dark mode should be active, which points to the token mismatch as the root cause.

This is a genuine host bug — the two theme systems are out of sync, with acrolls binding to --foreground (which resolved dark) while the body renders using --bg/--text-primary (which resolved light). Still, it's fair to note acrolls should bind background and foreground together on the same surface rather than relying on a single variable.

For the test deployment, I'm scoping a minimal fix: pinning the acrolls tokens to the host's actual body token family so the docs rendering matches what's really displayed on the page.

Now I need to check what other tokens exist in _tokens.sass — muted, secondary text, borders — so I can map the rest of the acrolls variables consistently. Let me grep for those.
]]></thinking>
Definitive root cause — and it&#39;s a **host bug acrolls surfaced**, not an acrolls bug. The host runs *two* token families that are out of sync:

- `body` uses `--bg` / `--text-primary` (from `_tokens.sass`) → resolving **light** (`#0f172a` text)
- `_colors.sass` defines a separate `--background` / `--foreground` family → resolving **dark** (`#f8f9fa`)

Acrolls binds to `--foreground`, so it inherited the dark-mode value onto a light-mode body. Let me align the docs surface with the family the body actually uses.

]]></arguments>
</tool_call>

]]></tool_body>

<tool_body><![CDATA[
@@ -1,3 +1,40 @@
-<DocsShell nav={docs.nav} pathname={page.url.pathname}>
-	{@render children()}
-</DocsShell>
+<div class="docs-surface">
+	<DocsShell nav={docs.nav} pathname={page.url.pathname}>
+		{@render children()}
+	</DocsShell>
+</div>
+
+<style>
+	/*
+	 * Bind Acrolls to the same token family `body` actually uses.
+	 *
+	 * Acrolls defaults its tokens to the shadcn-style `--foreground` / `--background`
+	 * pair from _colors.sass, but this site paints `body` from the `--text-primary` /
+	 * `--bg` pair in _tokens.sass. The two families resolve their dark-mode overrides
+	 * under different guards, so on a dark-preferring OS `--foreground` flips to
+	 * near-white while `--bg` stays light — which rendered the docs as near-white text
+	 * on a near-white page. Pinning Acrolls to the body's own family makes the docs
+	 * surface self-consistent in both modes by construction.
+	 */
+	.docs-surface {
+		--acrolls-foreground: var(--text-primary);
+		--acrolls-background: var(--bg);
+		--acrolls-muted: var(--text-secondary);
+		--acrolls-surface: var(--bg-surface);
+		--acrolls-surface-strong: var(--bg-raised);
+
+		--acrolls-docs-fg: var(--text-primary);
+		--acrolls-docs-muted: var(--text-secondary);
+		--acrolls-docs-surface: var(--bg-surface);
+		--acrolls-docs-border: color-mix(in oklab, var(--text-primary) 14%, transparent);
+		--acrolls-border: color-mix(in oklab, var(--text-primary) 14%, transparent);
+
+		background: var(--bg);
+		color: var(--text-primary);
+	}
+
+	/* The host's global link colour is themed for the other token family. */
+	.docs-surface :global(a) {
+		color: inherit;
+	}
+</style>
]]></tool_body>

<thinking><![CDATA[
The `.docs-surface` element is missing from the DOM entirely, which is strange since my wrapper div should have rendered around DocsShell. I'm wondering if Svelte's scoped styling somehow stripped the class, though that shouldn't affect the class attribute itself—just adding a hash suffix to styles.

Maybe the build didn't pick up my change, or the browser cached the old page, or DocsShell uses some kind of portal that renders elsewhere in the DOM. I also confirm my earlier diagnosis: the token families disagree on dark mode—`_colors.sass` applies its dark override while `_tokens.sass` doesn't, so binding to `--text-primary`/`--bg` correctly gives light-mode values for readable text. Now I just need to verify why my wrapper isn't showing up by inspecting the DOM directly.
]]></thinking>

Looking closer, the article background matches the host's dark background value exactly, which tells me acrolls must be redefining its own background token somewhere else, likely in a second block in docs.css (possibly a dark-mode media query) that's re-declaring these custom properties after my override and winning the cascade.

But custom property inheritance means a declaration on `.docs-surface` should beat one on `:root` inside a media query — unless acrolls is setting these tokens directly on `.acrolls` or `.acrolls-docs-shell`, which are descendants and would take precedence. I need to check where that second block in docs.css is actually scoped to confirm this, since if acrolls hardcodes tokens on its own root classes, that's a real theming limitation worth flagging.
]]></thinking>

<message role="assistant" ordinal="208" timestamp="2026-08-14T19:14:23.346Z" has_thinking="true" has_tool_use="true">
<thinking><![CDATA[
This confirms a real theming design flaw: both the `.acrolls` base class and the docs sidebar class redeclare their own CSS custom properties on themselves rather than inheriting from a shared scope, which means these local redeclarations override any values set higher up the tree.

Since the element's own declaration always wins over an ancestor override, the documented "set `--acrolls-*` variables on a host wrapper" approach simply can't work — I'd need to target the acrolls elements directly, or better, fix the underlying `--foreground` token mismatch at the host level so the fallback chain resolves correctly everywhere at once.
]]></thinking>
Confirmed — and this is a **genuine acrolls theming flaw worth reporting**. Both `.acrolls` and `.acrolls-docs-sidebar` *redeclare* the tokens on themselves:
<code_block language="css"><![CDATA[
.acrolls-docs-sidebar:not(...) { --acrolls-docs-fg: var(--foreground, var(--text-primary, currentColor)); }
]]></code_block>
An element&#39;s own declaration always beats an ancestor&#39;s, so the documented &#34;set `--acrolls-*` on a wrapper&#34; escape hatch **cannot work**. But the fallback chain gives a clean way in: override the *host* tokens acrolls reads, not acrolls&#39; own.

<message role="assistant" ordinal="244" timestamp="2026-08-14T19:18:10.359Z">
**0 errors.** The test deployment is live and working. The 2 remaining warnings are pre-existing, in the host&#39;s `DailyLogViewer` — unrelated to acrolls.

**Running at http://localhost:4173/docs**

## What was deployed

Installed via `pnpm pack` → tarball, not a workspace symlink — so this tested the **real published-consumer install path**, including bundled `@acrolls/*` runtime and the `exports` map. The new `content()` API is what&#39;s wired:
<code_block language="ts"><![CDATA[
export const docs = content({
  loader: markdownGlob({ body: …, modules: …, root: '../../docs' }),
  schema: frontmatter,          // Valibot, typed
  filter: (entry) => !entry.data.draft,
  config: defineDocsConfig({ … })
}).sourceSync();
]]></code_block>
Verified: **60/60 sampled documents return 200**, all 307 in the nav, Shiki code frames with Wrap/Copy, styled tables, breadcrumbs, pager, accordion sidebar, diacritics intact (Ṛg, maṇḍala, sūktas), and 404s on unknown slugs.

## Corpus preflight on a real corpus
<code_block><![CDATA[
307 discovered · 304 ready · 1 normalized · 2 rejected
]]></code_block>
This is the feature working as designed — two files have genuine mdsvex compile errors (indented SASS in fenced blocks) and didn&#39;t take down the other 305.

## Four real findings

**1. mdsvex breaks in runes-mode hosts.** mdsvex emits `&lt;Layout {...$$props}&gt;`, and `$$props` is illegal in runes mode. Your config forced `runes: true` on everything outside `node_modules`, which includes compiled `.md`. **Every markdown file failed to build.** Fixed by exempting `.md`/`.svx` in `vite.config.ts`. Acrolls&#39; docs don&#39;t mention this, and it will hit any runes-forced host — worth adding to the onboarding checkpoints.

**2. Acrolls&#39; host-theming escape hatch doesn&#39;t work as documented.** `.acrolls` and `.acrolls-docs-sidebar` *redeclare* their own tokens on themselves:
<code_block language="css"><![CDATA[
.acrolls-docs-sidebar:not(…) { --acrolls-docs-fg: var(--foreground, …); }
]]></code_block>
An element&#39;s own declaration always beats an ancestor&#39;s, so setting `--acrolls-*` on a wrapper — the documented approach — is silently ignored. The workaround is to override the *upstream host* tokens (`--foreground`, `--card`, `--border`) instead. This is a genuine acrolls bug.

**3. `validate` and the build disagree.** The CLI rejected `projectcrafting.md` and `stackanddesign.md`, but both compile fine in the host build and serve 200. That contradicts acrolls&#39; own success criterion 8 (&#34;directory validation and host preprocessing agree on the same compile result&#34;).

**4. `DocsPageHeader` + `PublicationLayout` double-render title and description.** Acrolls&#39; own example host has the same shape. I dropped the header and let the Banner do it.

## Two things I changed beyond acrolls wiring

- **Fixed a pre-existing host bug**: `src/routes/daily-logs/+page.svelte` imported `$lib/comps/…` instead of `$lib/components/…`. It was breaking your build before I arrived; I fixed it because it blocked the deployment.
- **Added `valibot`** as a devDependency for the schema.

Your site isn&#39;t a git repo, so backups of the three files I modified are at `scratchpad/host-backup/`. Worth running `git init` before going further.

Want me to fix the two acrolls bugs (#2 and #3) back in the monorepo, or leave the test deployment as the finding and stop here?
</message>

<message role="assistant" ordinal="246" timestamp="2026-08-14T19:53:35.593Z" has_thinking="true">
<thinking><![CDATA[
This is a fair question to sit with honestly rather than defensively. Looking at the four bugs — the mdsvex runes-mode break, the theming escape hatch failing, the validate/build mismatch, and the double-render issue — I'm realizing none of these actually live in the content layer, which is likely why verification missed them.

My verification was scoped to "did the façade preserve the engine's behavior?" and it did, but that's a self-referential test. Every check ran against acrolls' own example host, which doesn't force runes mode, has clean curated frontmatter, and uses default tokens — so it never exercised the adversarial conditions that would've surfaced these bugs. The nav-parity proof being byte-identical was real evidence that old==new, but if both were broken the same way, that parity stays silent — it proved the façade was behavior-preserving, not that the underlying behavior was correct.

The double-title bug was actually visible in the example's own source file, which I read and copied, but never caught because I only verified via grep/curl/build-exit-code rather than actually rendering anything in a browser — checking for presence of markup, not correctness of appearance. The theming, validate-vs-build, and compiler-options findings all required host conditions the example simply didn't have. The deeper pattern is that I verified the diff, not the product — and the acceptance criteria I used came from acrolls' own success criteria, which share the same blind spots.

I also designed the whole parallel-agent structure and wrote every task spec myself, so the agents only found what I pointed them at. Tellingly, the two real bugs that did surface came from moments where an agent touched actual reality — a real Valibot schema, real build output — rather than my spec, which in hindsight was a signal that my verification model was systematically weak, not just a one-off correction. And for a library whose entire value is visual typography, I never once looked at it rendered in a browser until now.

I don't want to overcorrect into excessive self-flagellation, but since this is a direct question deserving an honest answer, I should stay analytical rather than apologetic. The real nuance is that some of these aren't regressions I caused — they're pre-existing issues outside my task's scope — but the genuine error was letting a narrow, accurate verification (like T7's verdict) imply broader confidence than it earned. I should also note concretely what would have caught each issue.

The sharpest point is that my only integration surface was an example host sharing the library's own assumptions, while acrolls' README explicitly pointed to a "third-host trial" as the real validation path — something I read in my first tool call but never acted on until asked. I want to structure the answer as: a direct admission of scope, what would have caught each finding, the two signals I misread, and what I'd change — kept tight, specific, and useful rather than groveling.

I'm also noting that all three phases of my work — the initial architecture review, the task specs, and the final check — shared the same blind spot: no contact with a real host, no visual verification.
]]></thinking>
Fair question. The honest answer is that my verification was designed to prove *my change was safe*, not to find out whether acrolls *works*. Those are different questions and I only ever asked the first one.

Concretely, three compounding mistakes:

**I used acrolls&#39; own example as my only integration surface.** `examples/kit-consumer` doesn&#39;t force runes mode, has clean curated frontmatter, has an `index.md`, has 5 files instead of 307, and has no design system of its own. It&#39;s a fixture built by the same people, sharing the same assumptions as the library. Every one of the four findings needed a host that *didn&#39;t* share those assumptions. Your repo did in about ten minutes what the example structurally could not do at all. And acrolls&#39; README — which I read in my first tool call — explicitly points at a &#34;third-host trial&#34; path. I didn&#39;t take it until you told me to.

**I leaned on differential testing and overread it.** The nav-parity proof came back byte-identical, and I presented that as the strongest evidence in the whole exercise. It was strong evidence for exactly one claim: old and new produce the same output. It is *silent* by construction when both sides are broken the same way. I let a true, narrow result carry a broader implication it didn&#39;t earn.

**I never looked at it.** Every check I ran was a grep, a curl, an exit code, or a class-name count in a built chunk. For a library whose entire product is publication-grade typography, I verified presence of markup and never once rendered a page until this task. That&#39;s how the double-rendered title survived — it was sitting in `DocumentPage.svelte` in acrolls&#39; own example, I read that file, and I *copied it into your host*. One screenshot killed it.

What would actually have caught each:

| Finding | Minimum thing that would have found it |
|---|---|
| `$$props` in runes mode | Any host with non-default `compilerOptions` |
| Theming escape hatch broken | Any host with its own design tokens |
| `validate` vs build disagree | Running both on a corpus containing invalid files |
| Double-rendered title | Looking at one page |

The part that bothers me most is that I had the signal and misread it. Two agents independently hit the `StandardSchemaV1` defect — the moment T4 passed a *real* Valibot schema instead of my hand-written stub, and the moment T6 paste-tested a *real* sample. T4 also disproved the bundle claim by grepping actual build output. Every defect found in that whole run came from something touching reality rather than touching my spec. I treated that as &#34;corrective wave complete&#34; and moved on, when it was telling me my verification model was systematically weak: my tests were written from the same understanding as my code, so they could only confirm it.

And T7&#39;s verdict — &#34;coherent and non-breaking&#34; — was accurate about what it tested and misleading in how confidently I relayed it. The acceptance criteria I gave T7 came from acrolls&#39; own PRODUCT.md, checked against acrolls&#39; own example. The loop never left the building.

For what it&#39;s worth on scope: none of these four are regressions from the `content()` work — the façade genuinely is behavior-preserving, and the engine is provably untouched. But that distinction is mostly irrelevant to you. You asked for a library that works, and I spent a lot of tokens proving a narrow property while four real defects sat in the paths I never exercised.

The cheap fix going forward is to make a real third host the acceptance gate rather than the victory lap — and to open a browser before saying anything renders correctly.
</message>

<message role="assistant" ordinal="248" timestamp="2026-08-14T19:59:18.228Z" has_thinking="true" has_tool_use="true">
<thinking><![CDATA[
This is exactly what acrolls' Foundation mode and content source are designed for, and the new content() API I built works perfectly as a pure data layer here. They'd need access to documents with their keys, slugs, hrefs, titles, descriptions, metadata, and ordering, plus the generated nav tree they can repurpose as raw data for their own custom navigation.

For TOC generation though, I'm not sure acrolls exposes headings directly — the DocsDocumentFacts type tracks frontmatter, leading H1, and links, but not a full heading list, even though a DocsTocItem type exists somewhere. I need to check whether there's a helper that extracts headings from the DOM after render or from the compiled module, and also whether they'd still want to pull in the foundation CSS for structural styling even while dropping DocsShell and the editorial preset.

I noticed the user already edited the layout to drop the foundation import and keep default plus docs styles instead, which is worth remembering. My plan is to keep the content() API as the data layer, expose plain serializable sections/pages/tree data for custom navigation, and figure out TOC generation myself — likely by extracting headings client-side from the rendered article DOM since rehype-slug adds heading IDs, unless acrolls has a built-in TOC builder I haven't found yet. I recall seeing an "ON THIS PAGE" TOC rendered by DocsShell in the browser, so acrolls must generate it somehow — I need to track down where that logic lives.
]]></thinking>
Good — that&#39;s a clean fit. Acrolls&#39; content source is already a pure data API (`documents`, `get`, `entries`, `nav` as plain serializable data), so you can drop `DocsShell` entirely and keep the parts that discover and load content. The one thing I need to check is where the TOC comes from, since that&#39;s the piece most likely to be locked inside the shell.

<tool_result><![CDATA[
import type { DocsTocItem } from './types.js';
import { slugify } from './nav-path.js';

export type TocScanOptions = {
	/** Root element to scan (article body) */
	root: ParentNode;
	minLevel?: number;
	maxLevel?: number;
	/** Assign missing ids on headings */
	ensureIds?: boolean;
};

/**
 * Collect h2–h6 (default) from a DOM subtree for on-page TOC.
 */
export function scanHeadings(options: TocScanOptions): DocsTocItem[] {
	const { root, minLevel = 2, maxLevel = 3, ensureIds = true } = options;
	const selector = Array.from({ length: maxLevel - minLevel + 1 }, (_, i) => `h${minLevel + i}`).join(
		','
	);
	const nodes = root.querySelectorAll<HTMLElement>(selector);
	const used = new Set<string>();
	const items: DocsTocItem[] = [];

	nodes.forEach((el) => {
		const tag = el.tagName.toLowerCase();
		const level = Number(tag.replace('h', ''));
		if (!Number.isFinite(level)) return;
		let id = el.id;
		const text = (el.innerText || el.textContent || '').replace(/\s+/g, ' ').trim();
		if (!text) return;
		if (!id && ensureIds) {
			let base = slugify(text) || 'section';
			id = base;
			let n = 2;
			while (used.has(id) || (typeof document !== 'undefined' && document.getElementById(id) && document.getElementById(id) !== el)) {
				id = `${base}-${n++}`;
			}
			el.id = id;
		}
		if (!id) return;
		used.add(id);
		items.push({ id, text, level });
	});

	return items;
}

	openIdsForPath,
	normalizePath,
	slugify,
	stableId
} from './nav.js';

export { scanHeadings } from './toc.js';
]]></tool_result>

This means they can build their own shell entirely on top of acrolls content, using these pure functions for logic while owning the presentation layer themselves. I'm noting the TOC scanning happens client-side after render, so it'll need an effect hook once the article mounts.

My plan is to rewrite the layout to strip out DocsShell and the editorial CSS, replacing it with a minimal wrapper, then build their own nav and TOC components using the acrolls data and helpers—keeping foundation.css as the optional structural layer since that matches what they're asking for.

But I realize they also want full control over the article body itself, not just the shell. Since the article still renders through acrolls' PublicationLayout with its own article wrapper and banner, I need to consider whether to keep that layout or offer a way to get raw compiled markdown so they can wrap the body entirely themselves, possibly using the standalone mdsvex preprocessor rather than the SvelteKit-integrated one.

Checking the preprocessor setup, I see the SvelteKit variant defaults to wrapping content in PublicationLayout, but passing a custom or false layout option lets me bypass that. The code-frame, table, and heading-slug enhancements live in the mdsvex pipeline itself, independent of layout, so dropping the layout wrapper while keeping the preprocessor gives them pure enhanced content HTML to style and wrap as they like — which seems like the cleanest way to treat acrolls purely as a content engine.

That said, dropping the layout also means losing the `.acrolls`-scoped wrapper class, so the foundation CSS won't apply unless they add that class back themselves — worth flagging as a tradeoff rather than assuming.

I'll go with keeping the mdsvex pipeline (Shiki, slugs, tables, GFM) but dropping the PublicationLayout and all acrolls CSS, giving them their own shell, nav, and toc components built on acrolls' headless data/helpers, and noting they can restore foundation.css by wrapping content in `.acrolls`. Now I'm sketching the files needed: the vite config preprocessor setup, the existing docs source module, and a new nav module exposing section/page data.

I need to verify whether mdsvex's `layout` option actually accepts `false` to disable layouts entirely, or whether I need to check how acrolls' own mdsvex options helper handles that case — might be safer to use their existing options creator instead.

Everything you need is already headless. Acrolls exports the nav/pager/breadcrumb/TOC logic as **pure functions** separate from its components:

- `flattenDocsNav`, `findActiveTrail`, `docsPager`, `buildDocsCrumbs`, `openIdsForPath`, `sectionShouldOpen`
- `scanHeadings({ root })` — builds the TOC from the rendered article DOM
- `readOpenState` / `writeOpenState` — accordion persistence

So you can delete `DocsShell` and keep the data. One thing to confirm — whether I can also strip the article wrapper, since you said you want the body too.

Acrolls scans the *rendered* DOM rather than the source, so call this\n * after the article has mounted (in an $effect), not during load.\n */\nexport function tocFrom(root: ParentNode, minLevel = 2, maxLevel = 3): DocsTocItem[] {\n\treturn scanHeadings({ root, minLevel, maxLevel, ensureIds: true });\n}\n"}

@@ -0,0 +1,81 @@
+/**
+ * Headless docs data.
+ *
+ * Everything here is plain serializable data or pure functions re-exported from Acrolls.
+ * No Acrolls components and no Acrolls CSS are involved — the shell, nav, TOC, and body
+ * markup are all ours. Acrolls' job is limited to: discovering the corpus, compiling each
+ * document, and deriving titles/sections/routes/order from it.
+ */
+import {
+	buildDocsCrumbs,
+	docsPager,
+	findActiveTrail,
+	flattenDocsNav,
+	openIdsForPath,
+	scanHeadings
+} from 'acrolls/docs';
+import type { DocsCrumb, DocsNavNode, DocsPagerLink, DocsTocItem } from 'acrolls/docs';
+import { docs } from './source';
+
+export { docs };
+export type { DocsCrumb, DocsNavNode, DocsPagerLink, DocsTocItem };
+
+/** Top-level sections, in configured order. `items` is the recursive page tree. */
+export const sections = docs.nav.sections;
+
+/** Every visible page as a flat list — useful for search, feeds, sitemaps. */
+export const allPages = flattenDocsNav(docs.nav);
+
+/** Corpus title/subtitle/base path, from defineDocsConfig. */
+export const site = {
+	title: docs.nav.title,
+	subtitle: docs.nav.subtitle,
+	baseHref: docs.nav.baseHref
+};
+
+/** Build/compile diagnostics for this corpus (schema failures, admission warnings). */
+export const diagnostics = docs.diagnostics;
+
+/** One document's metadata record, or undefined. Accepts slug, href, or source key. */
+export function getDoc(slug: string) {
+	return docs.get(slug);
+}
+
+/** Lazily load one document's compiled Svelte component. */
+export function loadDoc(slug: string) {
+	return docs.load(slug);
+}
+
+/** Every routeable href — for prerender entries or a sitemap. */
+export function allHrefs(): string[] {
+	return docs.entries();
+}
+
+/** Previous/next links for a pathname, following configured order. */
+export function pager(pathname: string): { prev: DocsPagerLink; next: DocsPagerLink } {
+	return docsPager(docs.nav, pathname);
+}
+
+/** Breadcrumb trail for a pathname. Last crumb is the current page. */
+export function crumbs(pathname: string): DocsCrumb[] {
+	return buildDocsCrumbs(docs.nav, pathname);
+}
+
+/** The chain of nav nodes leading to the active page — for highlighting an open path. */
+export function activeTrail(pathname: string) {
+	return findActiveTrail(docs.nav, pathname);
+}
+
+/** Node ids that should start open so the active page is visible. */
+export function openIds(pathname: string): string[] {
+	return openIdsForPath(docs.nav, pathname);
+}
+
+/**
+ * On-page TOC. Acrolls scans the *rendered* DOM rather than the source, so call this
+ * after the article has mounted (in an $effect), not during load.
+ */
+export function tocFrom(root: ParentNode, minLevel = 2, maxLevel = 3): DocsTocItem[] {
+	return scanHeadings({ root, minLevel, maxLevel, ensureIds: true });
+}
+
]]></tool_body>

'page' : undefined}\u003e\n\t\t\t\t\t\t{item.title}\n\t\t\t\t\t\u003c/a\u003e\n\t\t\t\t{:else}\n\t\t\t\t\t\u003cspan\u003e{item.title}\u003c/span\u003e\n\t\t\t\t{/if}\n\n\t\t\t\t{#if item.children?.length}\n\t\t\t\t\t{@render nodes(item.children)}\n\t\t\t\t{/if}\n\t\t\t\u003c/li\u003e\n\t\t{/each}\n\t\u003c/ul\u003e\n{/snippet}\n\n\u003cnav aria-label=\"Documentation\"\u003e\n\t{#each sections as section (section.id)}\n\t\t\u003cdetails open={open.has(section.id) || section.defaultOpen !== false}\u003e\n\t\t\t\u003csummary\u003e{section.title}\u003c/summary\u003e\n\t\t\t{@render nodes(section.items)}\n\t\t\u003c/details\u003e\n\t{/each}\n\u003c/nav\u003e\n\n\u003c!-- Skeleton only — replace freely. Structure and hooks are what matter. --\u003e\n\u003cstyle\u003e\n\tnav ul {\n\t\tlist-style: none;\n\t\tmargin: 0;\n\t\tpadding-left: 0.75rem;\n\t}\n\tnav a {\n\t\tdisplay: block;\n\t\tpadding: 0.15rem 0;\n\t\tcolor: inherit;\n\t\ttext-decoration: none;\n\t}\n\tnav a[aria-current='page'] {\n\t\tfont-weight: 600;\n\t}\n\tsummary {\n\t\tcursor: pointer;\n\t\tfont-weight: 600;\n\t\tpadding: 0.35rem 0;\n\t}\n\u003c/style\u003e\n"}

@@ -0,0 +1,66 @@
+<script lang="ts">
+	import type { DocsNavNode } from './content';
+	import { sections, openIds } from './content';
+
+	let { pathname }: { pathname: string } = $props();
+
+	// Which groups start open so the active page is visible.
+	const open = $derived(new Set(openIds(pathname)));
+
+	function isActive(href: string | undefined) {
+		return !!href && href === pathname;
+	}
+</script>
+
+{#snippet nodes(items: DocsNavNode[])}
+	<ul>
+		{#each items as item (item.id)}
+			<li>
+				{#if item.href}
+					<a href={item.href} aria-current={isActive(item.href) ? 'page' : undefined}>
+						{item.title}
+					</a>
+				{:else}
+					<span>{item.title}</span>
+				{/if}
+
+				{#if item.children?.length}
+					{@render nodes(item.children)}
+				{/if}
+			</li>
+		{/each}
+	</ul>
+{/snippet}
+
+<nav aria-label="Documentation">
+	{#each sections as section (section.id)}
+		<details open={open.has(section.id) || section.defaultOpen !== false}>
+			<summary>{section.title}</summary>
+			{@render nodes(section.items)}
+		</details>
+	{/each}
+</nav>
+
+<!-- Skeleton only — replace freely. Structure and hooks are what matter. -->
+<style>
+	nav ul {
+		list-style: none;
+		margin: 0;
+		padding-left: 0.75rem;
+	}
+	nav a {
+		display: block;
+		padding: 0.15rem 0;
+		color: inherit;
+		text-decoration: none;
+	}
+	nav a[aria-current='page'] {
+		font-weight: 600;
+	}
+	summary {
+		cursor: pointer;
+		font-weight: 600;
+		padding: 0.35rem 0;
+	}
+</style>
+
]]></tool_body>

@@ -0,0 +1,56 @@
+<script lang="ts">
+	import type { DocsTocItem } from './content';
+	import { tocFrom } from './content';
+
+	// `root` is the rendered article element. Acrolls scans the DOM, so the TOC can only
+	// be built after the article mounts — and must be rebuilt when the document changes.
+	let { root, key }: { root: HTMLElement | undefined; key?: string } = $props();
+
+	let items = $state<DocsTocItem[]>([]);
+
+	$effect(() => {
+		// Touch `key` so switching documents re-scans even if `root` is reused.
+		void key;
+		if (!root) {
+			items = [];
+			return;
+		}
+		items = tocFrom(root);
+	});
+</script>
+
+{#if items.length}
+	<nav aria-label="On this page">
+		<p class="toc-title">On this page</p>
+		<ul>
+			{#each items as item (item.id)}
+				<li data-level={item.level}>
+					<a href="#{item.id}">{item.text}</a>
+				</li>
+			{/each}
+		</ul>
+	</nav>
+{/if}
+
+<!-- Skeleton only — replace freely. -->
+<style>
+	.toc-title {
+		font-weight: 600;
+		margin: 0 0 0.5rem;
+	}
+	ul {
+		list-style: none;
+		margin: 0;
+		padding: 0;
+	}
+	li[data-level='3'] {
+		padding-left: 0.75rem;
+	}
+	a {
+		color: inherit;
+		text-decoration: none;
+		display: block;
+		padding: 0.15rem 0;
+	}
+</style>
+
]]></tool_body>

<tool_body><![CDATA[
@@ -0,0 +1,100 @@
+<script lang="ts">
+	import Toc from './Toc.svelte';
+	import { crumbs, getDoc, pager } from './content';
+
+	let { slug, pathname }: { slug: string; pathname: string } = $props();
+
+	const doc = $derived(getDoc(slug));
+	const trail = $derived(crumbs(pathname));
+	const links = $derived(pager(pathname));
+
+	// Bound to the article element so the TOC can scan the rendered headings.
+	let articleEl = $state<HTMLElement>();
+</script>
+
+<svelte:head>
+	<title>{doc?.title ?? 'Docs'} · Fractal Mandala</title>
+	{#if doc?.description}
+		<meta name="description" content={doc.description} />
+	{/if}
+</svelte:head>
+
+{#if doc}
+	<div class="doc-layout">
+		<div class="doc-main">
+			<nav aria-label="Breadcrumb">
+				{#each trail as crumb, i (crumb.label + i)}
+					{#if crumb.href}<a href={crumb.href}>{crumb.label}</a>{:else}<span>{crumb.label}</span>{/if}
+					{#if i < trail.length - 1}<span aria-hidden="true"> / </span>{/if}
+				{/each}
+			</nav>
+
+			<!-- Our own page header, from Acrolls' derived metadata. -->
+			<header>
+				<h1>{doc.title}</h1>
+				{#if doc.description}<p class="doc-description">{doc.description}</p>{/if}
+			</header>
+
+			<!--
+				The compiled document. No PublicationLayout wrapper: content comes through
+				bare, with Acrolls' compile-time work intact (heading ids/anchors, Shiki
+				code frames, wrapped tables). `.doc-body` is ours to style.
+			-->
+			<article bind:this={articleEl} class="doc-body">
+				{#await doc.loader()}
+					<p>Loading…</p>
+				{:then Article}
+					<Article />
+				{:catch error}
+					<p class="doc-error">
+						This document could not be rendered: {error instanceof Error
+							? error.message
+							: String(error)}
+					</p>
+				{/await}
+			</article>
+
+			{#if links.prev || links.next}
+				<nav class="doc-pager" aria-label="Pagination">
+					{#if links.prev}<a href={links.prev.href}>← {links.prev.title}</a>{/if}
+					{#if links.next}<a href={links.next.href}>{links.next.title} →</a>{/if}
+				</nav>
+			{/if}
+		</div>
+
+		<aside class="doc-toc">
+			<Toc root={articleEl} key={slug} />
+		</aside>
+	</div>
+{:else}
+	<p>Document not found.</p>
+{/if}
+
+<!-- Skeleton only — replace freely. -->
+<style>
+	.doc-layout {
+		display: grid;
+		grid-template-columns: minmax(0, 1fr) 16rem;
+		gap: 2rem;
+		align-items: start;
+	}
+	.doc-toc {
+		position: sticky;
+		top: 1rem;
+	}
+	.doc-pager {
+		display: flex;
+		justify-content: space-between;
+		gap: 1rem;
+		margin-top: 2rem;
+	}
+	@media (max-width: 60rem) {
+		.doc-layout {
+			grid-template-columns: minmax(0, 1fr);
+		}
+		.doc-toc {
+			display: none;
+		}
+	}
+</style>
+
]]></tool_body>

<tool_body><![CDATA[
@@ -0,0 +1,61 @@
+<script lang="ts">
+	import type { Snippet } from 'svelte';
+	import { page } from '$app/state';
+	import Nav from '$lib/docs/Nav.svelte';
+	import { site } from '$lib/docs/content';
+
+	// No Acrolls components and no Acrolls CSS are imported anywhere in this surface.
+	// Acrolls is used purely as the content engine; this shell is ours.
+	let { children }: { children: Snippet } = $props();
+</script>
+
+<div class="docs-shell">
+	<aside class="docs-sidebar">
+		<a class="docs-brand" href={site.baseHref}>{site.title}</a>
+		{#if site.subtitle}<p class="docs-subtitle">{site.subtitle}</p>{/if}
+		<Nav pathname={page.url.pathname} />
+	</aside>
+
+	<main class="docs-content">
+		{@render children()}
+	</main>
+</div>
+
+<!-- Skeleton only — replace freely. -->
+<style>
+	.docs-shell {
+		display: grid;
+		grid-template-columns: 16rem minmax(0, 1fr);
+		gap: 2rem;
+		align-items: start;
+		max-width: 90rem;
+		margin: 0 auto;
+		padding: 1rem;
+	}
+	.docs-sidebar {
+		position: sticky;
+		top: 1rem;
+		max-height: calc(100dvh - 2rem);
+		overflow-y: auto;
+	}
+	.docs-brand {
+		font-weight: 700;
+		color: inherit;
+		text-decoration: none;
+	}
+	.docs-subtitle {
+		margin: 0.25rem 0 1rem;
+		opacity: 0.7;
+		font-size: 0.85rem;
+	}
+	@media (max-width: 60rem) {
+		.docs-shell {
+			grid-template-columns: minmax(0, 1fr);
+		}
+		.docs-sidebar {
+			position: static;
+			max-height: none;
+		}
+	}
+</style>
+
]]></tool_body>
