export {
	compileDiagnostic,
	diagnosticError,
	renderInvalidDocumentModule,
	safetyFindingDiagnostic
} from './document-diagnostics.js';
export type {
	AcrollsDocumentDiagnostic,
	AcrollsDocumentMode,
	AcrollsDocumentStatus,
	AcrollsInvalidDocumentPolicy
} from './document-diagnostics.js';
export { renderAcrollsArticleHtml } from './render-html.js';
export type { RenderHtmlResult } from './render-html.js';
export { splitFrontmatter, renderBannerHtml } from './frontmatter.js';
export type { Frontmatter } from './frontmatter.js';
export { inspectAcrollsDocument, suppressInitialMarkdownH1 } from './document-facts.js';
export type { AcrollsDocumentFacts } from './document-facts.js';

export type AcrollsMdsvexOptions = HighlightOptions & {
  /** Path to Publication layout (host or package). */
	layout?: string | Record<string, string>;
	extensions?: string[];
	/** What to do when a Markdown document cannot compile. */
	onInvalidDocument?: AcrollsInvalidDocumentPolicy;
	/** Receive source and compiler diagnostics without changing the default behavior. */
	onDiagnostic?: (diagnostic: AcrollsDocumentDiagnostic) => void;
	/** Opt-in contract for a generated authored Markdown docs corpus. */
	docs?: {
		mode?: 'authored' | 'migration';
		leadingH1?: 'suppress-and-warn' | 'preserve';
	};
};

/**
 * Options object for mdsvex(...).
 * Layout should point at a Svelte component that wraps slot content in Publication.
 */
export function createAcrollsMdsvexOptions(options: AcrollsMdsvexOptions = {}) {
  const {
    strict = false,
    layout,
    extensions = ['.svx', '.md']
  } = options;

  return {
    extensions,
    layout,
    remarkPlugins: [remarkGfm, remarkAcrollsMermaidGuard],
    rehypePlugins: [rehypeSlug, rehypeAcrollsHeadingAnchors, rehypeAcrollsTableWrap],
    highlight: {
      highlighter: createAcrollsHighlighter({ strict })
    }
  };
}
]]></tool_result>

<message role="assistant" ordinal="322" timestamp="2026-08-14T20:06:26.673Z" has_thinking="true" has_tool_use="true">
<thinking><![CDATA[
I found the culprit: acrolls' own `openIdsForPath` helper pushes any section with `defaultOpen: true`, and since acrolls defaults all folders to that, it returns all 7 ids regardless of my fix. So I need to stop relying on that helper and instead compute the active section myself, using something like `findActiveSection` or `nodeContainsPath`.

Separately, I'm now digging into why the TOC shows 0 items despite the doc having an h2 heading. The real issue seems to be timing: the article's content loads asynchronously inside an `{#await}` block, so `articleEl` binds to the empty `<article>` element before the content actually renders, and my scanning effect never re-runs once the await resolves since its dependencies don't change.

To fix this, I'll restructure DocumentPage to resolve the loaded component into reactive state first, then have the TOC effect key off that resolved state so it scans only after content is actually present. I'll keep the heading scan limited to h2–h3 since those are the structurally meaningful levels across the corpus, accepting that docs using only h5 headings will simply show no TOC.
]]></thinking>
Both root-caused:

1. **`openIdsForPath` defeats collapsing by design** — acrolls&#39; own helper has `else if (section.defaultOpen) ids.push(section.id)`, and acrolls sets `defaultOpen: true` on every folder. So it always returns all 7. I need to compute the active section myself.
2. **The TOC scans an empty article.** `articleEl` binds immediately, but its content arrives after the `{#await}` resolves, and neither `root` nor `key` changes at that moment — so it scans nothing.

Your corpus uses h2 (804) and h3 (333) structurally, plus h5 (518) decoratively — so h2–h3 is the right scan range.

+/** The single section containing the current page, or undefined. */

<message role="assistant" ordinal="356" timestamp="2026-08-14T20:09:27.652Z" has_thinking="true" has_tool_use="true">
<thinking><![CDATA[
The heading itself looks perfectly scannable, so the scan function should find it — meaning the effect must not be running or is getting cancelled before it completes. I suspect the root binding passed as a prop might not be ready when the effect first fires, since it's bound via `bind:this` in the parent component.

I'm thinking it's cleaner to drop the rAF approach entirely and instead use `tick()` inside the effect to wait for the DOM to flush before scanning, with a cancellation flag to guard against stale updates. That should be more deterministic than guessing with animation frames — I'll test whether manually triggering the scan actually populates the items to confirm the effect logic itself is sound.

Looking closer at the Toc component's effect, I notice it resets items to empty when not ready, then schedules a frame to populate them once ready — nothing in there reads `items` itself, so there's no obvious feedback loop. The real suspect might be upstream: if the parent's effect that loads `Article` keeps re-running and resetting `Article` to undefined before setting it again, that would make `ready` flicker and explain the instability I'm seeing.

The nav markup shows an empty items array, meaning the `{#if}` block is false — so tocFrom either never ran or returned nothing. Tracing through scanHeadings, it should find the h2 and build a valid item, so the failure must be that `root` itself is undefined when the rAF fires — maybe the `bind:this` on the article element isn't wired up the way I expect.

Rather than keep theorizing, I should just restructure so DocumentPage computes tocItems itself right after the article is set, using tick() instead of relying on prop timing — that removes the whole race condition. I'll also widen the heading levels to h2–h5 since this corpus uses h5 headings frequently, which would give a much richer TOC with more entries.
]]></thinking>
The heading is perfectly scannable (visible, has text, has an id, matches the selector) — so the scan itself is fine; the prop-timing between the two components is the problem. Simplifying: compute the TOC in the parent right after the body mounts, removing the cross-component timing entirely.

<tool_body><![CDATA[
@@ -0,0 +1,48 @@
+<script lang="ts">
+	import type { DocsTocItem } from './content';
+
+	// Presentational only. The scan happens in DocumentPage, where the article body's
+	// mount timing is known — passing an element across a component boundary and trying
+	// to guess when its children exist is what made this silently empty before.
+	let { items }: { items: DocsTocItem[] } = $props();
+</script>
+
+{#if items.length}
+	<nav aria-label="On this page">
+		<p class="toc-title">On this page</p>
+		<ul>
+			{#each items as item (item.id)}
+				<li data-level={item.level}>
+					<a href="#{item.id}">{item.text}</a>
+				</li>
+			{/each}
+		</ul>
+	</nav>
+{/if}
+
+<!-- Skeleton only — replace freely. -->
+<style>
+	.toc-title {
+		font-weight: 600;
+		margin: 0 0 0.5rem;
+	}
+	ul {
+		list-style: none;
+		margin: 0;
+		padding: 0;
+	}
+	li[data-level='3'] {
+		padding-left: 0.75rem;
+	}
+	li[data-level='4'],
+	li[data-level='5'] {
+		padding-left: 1.5rem;
+	}
+	a {
+		color: inherit;
+		text-decoration: none;
+		display: block;
+		padding: 0.15rem 0;
+	}
+</style>
+
]]></tool_body>

$ ok=0; fail=0; failed=""
for f in $(find src/docs -name "*.md" | awk 'NR % 4 == 1' | head -75); do

done

<message role="assistant" ordinal="384" timestamp="2026-08-14T20:12:26.168Z">
Done. Acrolls is now a pure content engine in your repo — **75/75 sampled routes 200, zero acrolls stylesheets loaded**, all markup yours.

## What acrolls still does

Discovery, compilation, and derived metadata only:

- **`src/lib/docs/source.ts`** — the `content()` collection: 307 docs, Valibot schema, `filter`
- **`src/lib/docs/content.ts`** — headless data layer: `sections`, `allPages`, `getDoc`, `crumbs`, `pager`, `activeSection`, `tocFrom`, `diagnostics`

Compile-time work you keep for free: heading ids + anchors, Shiki code frames, wrapped tables, GFM, frontmatter, source-safety normalization.

## What&#39;s yours

`Nav.svelte`, `Toc.svelte`, `DocumentPage.svelte`, `+layout.svelte`, `+page.svelte` — plain semantic HTML with skeleton CSS marked *&#34;replace freely.&#34;* No `DocsShell`, no acrolls CSS imports, and I dropped `PublicationLayout` from the preprocessor so documents compile to bare content with no wrapper or auto-Banner.

**Sections now collapse** — only the active one opens. Set `open={false}` in `Nav.svelte:38` for strictly-always-collapsed.

## Three things I hit, worth knowing

**Acrolls&#39; `openIdsForPath` can&#39;t collapse.** It ends with `else if (section.defaultOpen) ids.push(section.id)`, and acrolls sets `defaultOpen: true` on every folder — so it always returned all 7. I use `findActiveSection` instead and left a note in `content.ts` so it doesn&#39;t bite you later.

**The TOC scans rendered DOM, not source.** That makes it timing-sensitive: my first version bound the article element before its content existed and silently produced an empty TOC. The scan now runs in `DocumentPage` after `tick()`, and `Toc.svelte` is purely presentational.

**TOC is h2–h3 only.** Your corpus has 804 h2 and 333 h3 used structurally, no h4, and 518 h5 used decoratively for pull-quotes. Scanning h5 filled the TOC with entire paragraphs. Docs that never used a real heading get no TOC — `DocumentPage.svelte:56` is the knob.

## One config change

I set `onInvalidDocument: &#39;error-page&#39;` and `docs: { mode: &#39;migration&#39; }` in `vite.config.ts`. Dropping `PublicationLayout` exposed that the base preprocessor defaults to **fail-fast**, so `stackanddesign.md` and `projectcrafting.md` killed the whole build. They now render as isolated diagnostic pages while the other 305 work. That also partly explains finding #3 from earlier — the validate/build disagreement depends on layout config, so it&#39;s a sharper bug report than I had before.

Everything typechecks (0 errors; the 2 warnings are pre-existing in `DailyLogViewer`). Running at **http://localhost:4173/docs**.
</message>

I need section landing pages for paths like /docs/writings and /docs/civilization, which currently 404 since there's no index.md in those folders. I'm weighing two approaches: adding index.md files so acrolls treats each folder as both a page and a group automatically, versus building a SvelteKit route that renders a generated index from the docs data instead.

The catch-all slug route already handles these paths but 404s since there's no matching entry, so the cleanest fix is modifying that catch-all to detect when a slug matches a known section and render a section index there rather than adding new routes or files — this works at any nesting depth with a single change.

So if they add an index.md file to that folder, the nav group automatically gets a landing link with no code changes needed — that's the native path. But I'll also implement the derived fallback so it works without requiring content: adding a helper to find a nav section by slug, updating the catch-all route to render a section listing page when no document exists but the slug matches a section, and creating a SectionPage component for that. I also need to make the section headers in the nav actually clickable links to these pages, while still preserving the expand/collapse toggle behavior.

I'm weighing whether to avoid the click conflict by putting the section link as a list item versus making the summary text itself clickable with click-stopPropagation — leaning toward the stopPropagation approach since it matches user expectations better, wrapping the link and disclosure triangle together inside the summary element.

Now I realize sections don't reliably carry an href or slug since there's no index.md — the section id is derived from a stable hash of the folder path, not the path itself, so I can't reconstruct the folder path from the section object alone. I need another way to map each section back to its folder path, possibly by deriving it from the documents' keys instead.

I'm thinking the cleanest approach is to build my own section index by grouping document slugs by their first path segment, then matching those against the folder titles from the config. Alternatively, I could infer the shared prefix across a section's item hrefs to recover the folder path that way.

My plan is to add a section index to content.ts with id, title, path, href, and pages, create a SectionPage component, update the slug route to handle section-level slugs, and make the nav link to section titles. I'll need to rebuild and preview to verify everything works before shutting the server down again as requested.
]]></thinking>
Two ways, and they compose — here&#39;s the distinction, then I&#39;ll implement the one that works without you writing any content.

**Option A — `index.md` per folder (acrolls-native).** Drop `src/docs/writings/index.md` and acrolls automatically treats it as that group&#39;s landing page: the route resolves, breadcrumbs work, and the nav group gets a landing link. Zero code. But it needs 7 files with real prose, and folders without one still 404.

**Option B — derived section pages.** Generate the section page from data you already have. No content files, always in sync, works for all 7 immediately.

I&#39;ll build B so it works now, and make it defer to A automatically if you later add an `index.md`.

'');\n\t\tif (!path) return null;\n\t\treturn {\n\t\t\tid: section.id,\n\t\t\ttitle: section.title,\n\t\t\tpath,\n\t\t\thref: `${docs.nav.baseHref.replace(/\\/$/, '')}/${path}`,\n\t\t\tlandingSlug: section.slug ?? null,\n\t\t\tpages: section.items\n\t\t} satisfies DocsSection;\n\t})\n\t.filter((s): s is DocsSection => s !== null);\n\n/** Look up a section by its path segment, e.g. \"writings\". */\nexport function getSection(path: string): DocsSection | undefined {\n\treturn docSections.find((s) => s.path === path);\n}"}

-/** Build/compile diagnostics for this corpus (schema failures, admission warnings). */
-export const diagnostics = docs.diagnostics;
+/** Build/compile diagnostics for this corpus (schema failures, admission warnings). */
+export const diagnostics = docs.diagnostics;
+
+/**
+ * Section landing pages.
+ *
+ * This corpus has no index.md anywhere, so Acrolls builds each folder as a navigation
+ * group with no landing page and refuses to invent one (it will not redirect a group to
+ * an arbitrary first child). These entries are derived instead, from data Acrolls already
+ * produced, so /docs/writings etc. resolve without authoring seven files.
+ *
+ * The folder path is recovered from the first child href rather than from `section.id`,
+ * because Acrolls hashes ids (`section-<stableId>`) and they are not path-derived.
+ *
+ * If you later add src/docs/<folder>/index.md, Acrolls picks it up as the real landing
+ * page and `landingSlug` becomes non-null — the route below prefers it automatically.
+ */
+export type DocsSection = {
+	id: string;
+	title: string;
+	/** Path segment, e.g. "writings". */
+	path: string;
+	href: string;
+	/** Set only when the folder has a real index.md landing document. */
+	landingSlug: string | null;
+	pages: DocsNavNode[];
+};
+
+function pathFromHref(href: string): string {
+	const base = docs.nav.baseHref.replace(/\/$/, '');
+	const rest = href.startsWith(base) ? href.slice(base.length) : href;
+	return rest.replace(/^\/+/, '').split('/')[0] ?? '';
+}
+
+export const docSections: DocsSection[] = docs.nav.sections
+	.map((section) => {
+		// A section's own href exists only when it has a landing document.
+		const path = section.href
+			? pathFromHref(section.href)
+			: pathFromHref(section.items.find((i) => i.href)?.href ?? '');
+		if (!path) return null;
+		return {
+			id: section.id,
+			title: section.title,
+			path,
+			href: `${docs.nav.baseHref.replace(/\/$/, '')}/${path}`,
+			landingSlug: section.slug ?? null,
+			pages: section.items
+		} satisfies DocsSection;
+	})
+	.filter((s): s is DocsSection => s !== null);
+
+/** Look up a section by its path segment, e.g. "writings". */
+export function getSection(path: string): DocsSection | undefined {
+	return docSections.find((s) => s.path === path);
+}
]]></tool_body>

<tool_body><![CDATA[
@@ -0,0 +1,64 @@
+<script lang="ts">
+	import { crumbs, getSection, site } from './content';
+
+	let { path, pathname }: { path: string; pathname: string } = $props();
+
+	const section = $derived(getSection(path));
+	const trail = $derived(crumbs(pathname));
+</script>
+
+<svelte:head>
+	<title>{section?.title ?? 'Section'} · {site.title}</title>
+	<meta
+		name="description"
+		content="{section?.pages.length ?? 0} pages in {section?.title ?? 'this section'}."
+	/>
+</svelte:head>
+
+{#if section}
+	<nav aria-label="Breadcrumb">
+		{#each trail as crumb, i (crumb.label + i)}
+			{#if crumb.href}<a href={crumb.href}>{crumb.label}</a>{:else}<span>{crumb.label}</span>{/if}
+			{#if i < trail.length - 1}<span aria-hidden="true"> / </span>{/if}
+		{/each}
+	</nav>
+
+	<header>
+		<h1>{section.title}</h1>
+		<p>{section.pages.length} pages</p>
+	</header>
+
+	<ul class="section-list">
+		{#each section.pages as item (item.id)}
+			<li>
+				{#if item.href}
+					<a href={item.href}>{item.title}</a>
+				{:else}
+					<span>{item.title}</span>
+				{/if}
+				{#if item.description}<p>{item.description}</p>{/if}
+			</li>
+		{/each}
+	</ul>
+{:else}
+	<p>Section not found.</p>
+{/if}
+
+<!-- Skeleton only — replace freely. -->
+<style>
+	.section-list {
+		list-style: none;
+		padding: 0;
+		display: grid;
+		gap: 1rem;
+	}
+	.section-list p {
+		margin: 0.15rem 0 0;
+		opacity: 0.7;
+		font-size: 0.9rem;
+	}
+	a {
+		color: inherit;
+	}
+</style>
+
]]></tool_body>

{"replace_all":false,"file_path":"/Users/amrit/fractalmandala/fractalmandala/src/lib/docs/content.ts","old_string":"/** Breadcrumb trail for a pathname. Last crumb is the current page. */\nexport function crumbs(pathname: string): DocsCrumb[] {\n\treturn buildDocsCrumbs(docs.nav, pathname);\n}","new_string":"/**\n * Breadcrumb trail for a pathname. Last crumb is the current page and stays unlinked.\n *\n * Acrolls leaves a folder crumb hrefless when that folder has no landing document, which\n * is every folder in this corpus. Since section pages are derived (see `docSections`),\n * the href is filled in here so the section is reachable from the breadcrumb.\n */\nexport function crumbs(pathname: string): DocsCrumb[] {\n\tconst trail = buildDocsCrumbs(docs.nav, pathname);\n\tconst last = trail.length - 1;\n\treturn trail.map((crumb, i) =\u003e {\n\t\tif (crumb.href || i === last) return crumb;\n\t\tconst match = docSections.find((s) =\u003e s.title === crumb.label);\n\t\treturn match ? { ...crumb, href: match.href } : crumb;\n\t});\n}"}

<tool_body><![CDATA[
@@ -1,4 +1,16 @@
-/** Breadcrumb trail for a pathname. Last crumb is the current page. */
-export function crumbs(pathname: string): DocsCrumb[] {
-	return buildDocsCrumbs(docs.nav, pathname);
-}
+/**
+ * Breadcrumb trail for a pathname. Last crumb is the current page and stays unlinked.
+ *
+ * Acrolls leaves a folder crumb hrefless when that folder has no landing document, which
+ * is every folder in this corpus. Since section pages are derived (see `docSections`),
+ * the href is filled in here so the section is reachable from the breadcrumb.
+ */
+export function crumbs(pathname: string): DocsCrumb[] {
+	const trail = buildDocsCrumbs(docs.nav, pathname);
+	const last = trail.length - 1;
+	return trail.map((crumb, i) => {
+		if (crumb.href || i === last) return crumb;
+		const match = docSections.find((s) => s.title === crumb.label);
+		return match ? { ...crumb, href: match.href } : crumb;
+	});
+}
]]></tool_body>

Append the section itself as the final, unlinked crumb.\n\tconst trail = $derived.by(() =\u003e {\n\t\tconst base = crumbs(pathname);\n\t\tif (!section) return base;\n\t\tif (base.at(-1)?.label === section.title) return base;\n\t\treturn [...base, { label: section.title }];\n\t});"}

-	const section = $derived(getSection(path));

+	const section = $derived(getSection(path));
+
+	// Acrolls doesn't know this path (the folder has no landing document), so its trail
+	// stops at the docs root. Append the section itself as the final, unlinked crumb.
+	const trail = $derived.by(() => {
+		const base = crumbs(pathname);
+		if (!section) return base;
+		if (base.at(-1)?.label === section.title) return base;
+		return [...base, { label: section.title }];
+	});

<message role="assistant" ordinal="458" timestamp="2026-08-14T21:08:17.582Z">
All 7 section pages live, 0 type errors.

## How section pages work

They&#39;re **derived**, not authored — no content files needed. Acrolls refuses to invent a landing page for a folder without `index.md` (it won&#39;t redirect a group to an arbitrary child), so I built them from data acrolls already produces.

**`content.ts`** — `docSections` derives each section&#39;s path, href, and pages. The folder path is recovered from the first child&#39;s href, because acrolls hashes section ids (`section-&lt;stableId&gt;`) and they aren&#39;t path-derived.

**`[...slug]/+page.ts`** resolves in priority order:
1. real document → `kind: &#39;document&#39;`
2. bare folder path → `kind: &#39;section&#39;`
3. otherwise → 404

That ordering is what makes Option A work later: **drop a `src/docs/writings/index.md` and it just takes over** — acrolls routes the folder to it, step 1 catches it, and the derived page steps aside. `landingSlug` on each section tells you which folders have one.

## Your three corrections

**Sidebar titles are plain text again.** You were right — an `&lt;a&gt;` inside `&lt;summary&gt;` both navigates and toggles, so it fought the accordion. Reverted with a comment so it doesn&#39;t get re-added.

**Section links moved to the breadcrumb.** Acrolls leaves that crumb hrefless because the folder has no landing document, so `crumbs()` now fills it in — never on the last crumb. On `/docs/civilization/dharma` you get `Home → / · Fractal Mandala → /docs · Civilization → /docs/civilization`. Section pages also append their own name as a final unlinked crumb.

**Sidebar only on item pages.** The layout keys off `page.data.kind === &#39;document&#39;`. Section pages and the `/docs` index go full-width; the grid switches to a single column when the sidebar is absent.

Cards use your existing `.card` from `_primitives.sass:180` — I didn&#39;t restyle it, just laid them out in `repeat(auto-fill, minmax(16rem, 1fr))`.

Preview is running again at **http://localhost:4173** since you&#39;re iterating. Say the word and I&#39;ll shut it down.
</message>
